## November 21, 2026 — RESHAPING RANK 0: FUNDAMENTAL TRANSMUTATION (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**All eligible Nymbrin alchemists have Reshaping Rank 0:** basic **Mold, Join/Separate and Displace** of nonliving matter, without purchasing a Reshaping specialty rank. Rank 0 has **no special small-quantity restriction**; legally purchased Magnitude can cover much larger amounts, with legal Channel Limit, circle capacity, material resistance, base spell cost, matter and Hex requirements. **Meaningful Reshaping always costs positive spell-equivalent MP** under Nymbrin Hex casting; ordinary 0-MP offensive cantrip scaling does **not** permit free large or dangerous transmutations. Inconsequential party tricks (bent spoon, folded tablecloth, simple shirt repair) may have negligible untracked Hex and cost, without meaningful combat, infrastructure, equipment sabotage or production use. Higher Reshaping ranks should unlock **new categories of technique**, not ordinary Magnitude quantity upgrades. No innate supernatural hardening/softening, sustained animation, Recomposition, Vital Transmutation or expert Quality manufacture from Rank 0. Existing structural Defense material benchmarks apply to legitimately affected portions; exact Reshaping Power-versus-resistance and prices remain OPEN. Workshop/urban Hex economics deferred. See [Wetcraft](20_Nymbrin_Wetcraft_and_Hexen_Canon.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 21, 2026 — OPPOSED CHECKS AND CIRCLE HACKING (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Universal Opposed Check:** use the existing **defender/attacker relative-statistic ratio** and six Naked-d20 difficulty categories as in Combat Speed hit/evasion; calculate numerical failure margin as `target minus rolled d20`. **Failing by 9 or more is a critical failure**, with the specific activity determining consequences. **Reactivity circle hacking:** trained **Autonomy III Reactivity** hacker's effective INT opposes maker's installation-time effective INT; failure **1–4 retry**, **5–8 lockout 10 minutes**, **9+ alarm if present plus lockout 24 hours**. All legal access/capacity rules still apply. **Round fractional Channel Limit and installed/foreign-circle capacity DOWN.** Haze entropy is another **independent sequential** loss, not an additive penalty that zeros allied ritual mana: 100 ally MP -> 50 cooperative -> 25 week-long -> 12.5 if also in Haze V before applicable final rounding. Existing specialist medium-seizure rules remain until explicitly migrated. Owners: [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Nymbrin Wetcraft](20_Nymbrin_Wetcraft_and_Hexen_Canon.md), [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 21, 2026 — HAZE ENTROPY AND ALCHEMICAL CIRCLE CHANNELING (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Haze IV destroys 25% of channeled mana**, and **Haze V destroys 50%**, before effective spell-cost qualification. Users can compensate by increasing raw throughput only within legal current action/ritual Channel Limit and resources (10-MP working, CL 14: possible in IV using 14 raw, impossible in V without 20 raw). This explains loss of function in otherwise physically intact circles. **Constructed circle capacity** is capped by its maker's applicable Channel Limit **at construction time**, including legitimate ritual multiplier where used; later users cannot simply increase installed capacity. Other alchemists may use an unprotected circle at **half its usable channel capacity** if its style is unfamiliar, returning to full after **ten minutes of communication/alignment**. Qualified **Autonomy III Reactivity** enables circles restricting authorized users. Existing ritual entropy, capacity, mana supply and physical circle vulnerability remain; exact multi-loss ordering, capacity rounding and user-security contests are OPEN. See [Nymbrin Wetcraft](20_Nymbrin_Wetcraft_and_Hexen_Canon.md) and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 21, 2026 — SCARLANDS HAZE AND PURITY RULES (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**Latest tier precedence:** Ambient Haze is **0–V**: clear (0), trace borders (I), polluted (II), hazardous industrial (III), critical creature collapse/circle failure (IV), saturated unbreathable/unshielded alchemy fails (V). Hex exposure builds the existing **Hexed** status, using a proposed level-10 implemented Nymbrin/half earned points into INT **zero-extra-MP Power** benchmark; exact tier multipliers are still testing candidates (I–V = 0.5/1/2/4/8). **Clean-air breathing removes 10% of applicable Hex Status Resistance in buildup each full turn**. Existing **clearbreath** respirators gain Leywell-powered purity sigils, ignoring **2 effective Haze tiers** while charged. **Purity Circles**, accessible inexpensively to alchemists, remove up to **2 local effective Haze levels** with **two free duration tiers**, but remain anchored and require lawful resources. Living alchemy may export Hexed buildup into the environment and separately improve lungs' Hex resistance. Exact numeric spell costs, mask Leywell drain, exposure intervals, neutralization/transport, and purification protection of circles at IV–V remain OPEN. This supersedes conflicting older unspecific Haze descriptions without erasing compatible scarland culture/sensitivity lore. See [Wetcraft](20_Nymbrin_Wetcraft_and_Hexen_Canon.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 21, 2026 — PURGING CROAK AND BATTLEFIELD HEX TILES APPROVED; HAZE ECOLOGY IN DESIGN

**Approved:** Nymbrin may croak for both turn actions and, if not wounded or stunned before their next turn, spend blue MP on completion to purge stored personal Hex **3:1**, while a ten-minute rest clears personal Hex without MP. Discharged Hex creates **one environmental tile per spell-equivalent MP** (under provisional 3 Hex per MP), placeable within lawful Circuit **Extended Range**; outdoors lasts **3 turns**, inside enclosed rooms **1 minute**. First entry per turn applies buildup of the established Hexed condition to anyone, **including Nymbrin**. Tile buildup magnitude remains OPEN. **Ecology under design:** Ambient Haze, distinct from fresh tiles, decays and is processed by living populations at health cost; ordinary regions clear, borderlands lighter, critical thresholds disrupt circles and vulnerable life, catastrophic saturation unbreathable. Species-specific nonabsorption and civic countermeasures remain proposals. See [Wetcraft](20_Nymbrin_Wetcraft_and_Hexen_Canon.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 21, 2026 — NYMBRIN HEX-CASTING AND CIRCUIT INFRASTRUCTURE (LATEST DESIGN DIRECTION)

**Author-selected test model:** Nymbrin use ordinary Channel Limit, but Wetcraft generates **3 personal Hex per 1 spell-equivalent MP** instead of directly spending personal blue MP; personal Hex may not exceed **maximum personal MP**. Ten minutes of rest releases accumulated personal Hex without personal mana cost, **not without real environmental pollution**. This creates a between-rest casting budget of `floor((max MP - current Hex)/3)` before other legal limits. **Sigil Scar:** willing healing may erase buildup *before activation* or remove an active scar if healing Power at least equals original scar spell Power, with **+20 percentage points Toxicity per scar removed** (plus normal wound-healing Toxicity for any actual wounds healed); status reduces **END-derived physical Defense only** down to floor 1, leaving armor contribution intact. The exact severity debuff coefficient remains OPEN. **Circuits:** large antenna/relay circles may carry real blue mana to distant anchored works across many miles; energy sources, relay losses, network security and pollution destinations remain unpriced design subjects. See [Nymbrin Wetcraft](20_Nymbrin_Wetcraft_and_Hexen_Canon.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 21, 2026 — NYMBRIN CIRCUITS AND SIGIL SCARS: DESIGN DIRECTION (LATEST)

**Author-selected direction, quantitative calibration OPEN:** Nymbrin alchemical circles have an interior working area and **Extended Range** for placing/connecting more circles; linked circles form a mana- and access-relaying **Circuit**. Magnitude governs circle scale, not added circle-operating reach. The next-higher circle can be established in one action at lawful reach and generates Hex. Anchors on affected matter require size relative to effect (two-category allowance proposed), normal spell Duration, unbuffed-substrate durability, and actual blue mana to refresh at the original spell MP price through legal Circuit access. Anchored **hardening/softening** use a provisional half-Power Defense rider (quarter-Power alternative). **Sigil Scar** is a proposed hostile living-material status with 1×/4× thresholds, 2× casting-Power buildup and healing-Power parity for removal, plus Toxicity; numeric debuff and scar-only Toxicity need testing. All relevant unfinished mechanics remain proposals or calibration directions, not a complete final ruleset. See [Nymbrin Wetcraft](20_Nymbrin_Wetcraft_and_Hexen_Canon.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 21, 2026 — NYMBRIN TRANSMUTATION PERSISTENCE: PHYSICAL STABILITY (LATEST)

**Author approved:** A lawful Nymbrin transmutation whose **finished physical result is independently stable** persists without an ongoing spell, separate stabilization procedure or intact casting circle. An **unstable configuration** instead needs a functioning **anchoring circle** to remain altered; losing that support brings physical reversion/settling subject to actual matter, obstacles and finite force, never a rewind of history. This does not grant free material, engineering knowledge, equipment Quality, bypassed smithing prerequisites or Hex-free work. Warlock Blue Morph's distinct temporary/reversion rules remain untouched. Exact circle size, connections, damage/maintenance, mana/Hex rates and technique progression remain OPEN. See [Nymbrin Wetcraft](20_Nymbrin_Wetcraft_and_Hexen_Canon.md) and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 21, 2026 — FLAT MAGICAL WOUND-HEALING TOXICITY (LATEST)

**Approved:** Magically healing **any wound** adds **+20 percentage points of cumulative Toxicity per wound actually healed**, including Shimmer Guard wounds, with no severity-based discount or surcharge. The old superficial/ordinary/Shimmer Guard tiered healing Toxicity schedule is **SUPERSEDED**. Other Toxicity sources (including mana potions), threshold wounds, and currently unresolved rest/healing-potion details remain unchanged. See [Combat Endurance and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 20, 2026 — FINAL FINISHED-ITEM PRICE COMPLETION (LATEST)

**Author-directed finishing pass:** The current [Gem Price Catalogue](37_Gem_Price_Catalogue.md) now also prices **all 36 existing generic weapon enchantments and Guardian shield effect conditionally on a legal completed installation**: for unenchanted host Gem price `P` and genuinely eligible occupied effect slots `s`, **Mixed final `P(1+0.15s)`**, **Full final `P(1+0.20s)`**. This implements approximately twice-host pricing for substantial enchantment without making new rune effects, mana/slot legality, MP bands, stats or crafting permissions. See [36_Weapon_Enchantments.md](36_Weapon_Enchantments.md).

**Additional finished equipment tariffs:** Previously unpriced **Quality I–IV** high-engineering High-draw/Titan/Colossal/Greatbow reference bows now cost **60/70/80/90% of their current established Quality-V** family rate (100% at QV). Quality-V **Advanced Crossbow 150,000**, **Advanced Arbalest 250,000**, **Light Precision Firearm 125,000**, **Advanced Precision Rifle 175,000 Gems**. Ordinary compatible replacement **12 standard crossbow bolts 20 Gems; 12 heavy arbalest bolts 40; 12 plain firearm cartridges 60**; generic arrows remain **40/30 Gems**. No new ranges, Power, magazine capacity or specialty ammunition. See [27_Ranged_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md](27_Ranged_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md).

**Magical Portable Forge:** **10,000 Gems**, for its already-approved substitution for a dedicated physical forge, **not** additional crafting powers, missing fuel, ingredients or spell exceptions. See [07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md](07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md). **Unpriced concepts remain clearly separate** where potency, construction or legality is not established: bespoke enchants, Blank-stat recipes, new alchemical/status potions, exotic ammo, constructs and custom manufacturing. Existing Gem payouts `B(L)=10L−3`, reference wealth and item effectiveness are unchanged. Full owner's notes: [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

**AUTHOR APPROVED ECONOMIC REBASE; new current price catalogue:** [37 — Gem Price Catalogue and Encounter Reward Calibration](37_Gem_Price_Catalogue.md). This **supersedes all incompatible older Gem numbers**, including the former wealth milestones `200/400/800/1600/3200`, ordinary Heavy finished QI–V `60/120/240/480/960`, historic Gem Shop prices and the outdated 6/12/24/48/96 mana-potion schedule. The item's **physical/magical power, stats, equipment Quality, prerequisites, effect, Soul Strain, MP, SP, crafting and material restrictions do not change**.

**Monster and quest earnings:** For patron-qualifying equal-level Henchman equivalents, `B(L)=10L−3` Gems at Hero level 1–100, giving **7/97/247/497/747/997 Gems** at levels 1/10/25/50/75/100. Horde ×.1, Minion ×.4, Henchman ×1 and Elite ×2 scale with qualifying **encounter level / Hero level**; GM-rated noncombat deeds use equivalent encounter awards, not automatic kills. Issue whole **Gems and Glints (100 Glints/Gem)** and carry sub-Glint accounting fractions. Boss XP still advances **one** level; its separate **two-level Gem budget** remains approved for `L+2 ≤ 100` until specifically revised.

**Reference equipment owned wealth:** Levels **1/10/25/50/75/100 ≈ 30 / 1,128 / 17,326 / 135,240 / 412,291 / 878,286 Gems** with 30% consumable gross allocation, eligible previously-shop-sold item 50% buyback, and smoothly accrued replacement reserve. Lifetime gross by level100 ≈**1,658,926.5 Gems**, consumption ≈**497,677.95** and replacement loss ≈**282,993**. This is reference power/quality affordability, **not free Gem grants**. QI–V ordinary Heavy two-handed/Full Heavy prices **200/600/10,000/80,000/250,000**; Medium one-handed/full armor and Medium/Heavy shields half; Light one-handed/full armor 40%; Light shield 25%; Full/Standard/Partial armor 100%/75%/50%. **Junk is half matching QI** (light/medium/heavy full weapon-or-armor **40/50/100**, light/medium-heavy shield **25/50**) with *all* unchanged Junk penalties. ## November 20, 2026 — BOSS XP ONE LEVEL; QUEST REWARDS BY ENCOUNTER EQUIVALENCE (LATEST)

**Author-approved correction:** A qualifying Boss now grants exactly the remaining XP needed to advance its Hero **one level** from current progress: at Hero level L with P progress toward the next level, `Boss XP = L × (L + 1) − P`. The former *two-level Boss XP* award is **SUPERSEDED**; all ordinary XP thresholds, threat multipliers, level-up attributes and patron qualification rules remain unchanged. **Boss Gems remain a separate two-level wealth-budget award** (`E(L+2)−E(L)`) unless the author later explicitly revises that Gem rule; no Gem/XP parity is implied for the Boss exception.

**Quest equivalence:** A DM may budget or retroactively assess patron-qualifying quests and combat-bypassing deeds by the **approximate number and threat of equivalent encounters**, awarding appropriate XP and Gems without requiring kills and without a compulsory quest/monster income split. Avoid double-counting the same challenge. **Whole currency already exists:** 100 integer **Glints = 1 Gem**, and sub-Glint theoretical remainders can be carried forward until a whole Glint or bundled milestone payout. No new numeric monster drop chart, rounding mandate or smooth-reserve proposal has been approved. Owning documents: [`01_World_and_Shimmer.md`](01_World_and_Shimmer.md) for XP and quest threat; [`09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md) for Gem budgets and denomination. See [`10_Canon_Changelog.md`](10_Canon_Changelog.md) for supersession.

## November 20, 2026 — UNIVERSAL MAGNITUDE MP AND PYROMANCY CONTAINMENT APPROVED (LATEST)

**Approved universal Magnitude area categories and MP prices:** **0 single target/1 MP; I adjacent targets/2 MP; II 20-foot burst/4 MP; III 100-foot burst/10 MP; IV 1,000-foot burst/30 MP; V 2-mile burst/60 MP.** These area-component costs are separate from base offensive Power MP and other legal modifiers. The old "provisional" label on this numeric chart is **SUPERSEDED**. The merged Magnitude discipline still combines placement Range, area and sustained-work multiplicity; exact remapping of earlier reach benchmarks, multiplicity scaling, and other component prices remains open.

**Pyromancy-only exception:** Pyromancy's natural offensive area expands without a separate paid expansion surcharge as offensive investment rises. **Magnitude investment permits paying to restrain/shrink the spontaneous blast**; containment costs **0/I/II/III/IV/V = 1/2/4/10/20/30 MP**, with discounted **IV 20** (universal 30) and **V 30** (universal 60). Even a master concentrating **81 fire tiles** into a **20-MP base-offensive spell** cannot shrink its effect below a **20-foot burst**. Natural fire spread can outgrow learned area ceilings but **does not increase placement Range**, and can catch its caster. Ignition requires real eligible fuel; no invented free medium or repeated full-Power magical injury. Exact MP/medium-to-natural-radius curve, per-rank containment limits and other geometric edge cases remain **OPEN**. Full owning rule: [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

Damage forms still affect armor penetration (Slashing 0%, Blunt 25%, Piercing 50%) and all applicable weapon rules. **Author approval supersedes earlier 'experimental' labels:** Ordinary offensive spell Power with neutral implement: `P = (effective governing attribute² + IR²) × M`; **0/1/2/4/8/12/20 base-offense MP → ×0.55/×1/×1.35/×1.70/×2.05/×2.25/×2.51**. Maximum **20 MP to base Power**, with separate legal area/Duration costs. **10-minute ritual capacity = 4× full applicable Channel Limit**, limited by actual mana/requirements and not an independent damage bonus. **All five approved structural Defense Power references:** wood **1,000**; thick wood/thin stone **8,000**; castle masonry **12,500**; reinforced stone/metal **40,000**; extreme nonmagical fortifications **80,000**. **Approved benchmark:** ordinary unenchanted castle masonry **structural Defense Power 12,500** under the proposed structure-wound adaptation. Desired ordinary-spell breakthrough progression: level 1 wood; level 25 thick wood/thin stone; level 50 ordinary castle masonry; level 75 reinforced stone/metal; level 100 highest ordinary unenchanted fortifications. **Only castle 12,500 is approved numerically.** Proposed other structural Defenses for calibration: wood 1,000; thick timber/thin stone 8,000; reinforced stone/metal 40,000; extreme nonmagical structure 80,000. ## November 20, 2026 — ELEMENTAL STATUS RESISTANCE FORMULA APPROVED (LATEST)

**Element-specific SR = ceil(((effective END + 0.65 × applicable MR)² × applicable elemental Resistance multiplier) / 0.70)**, rounding **once at the end**; use elemental Resistance multiplier 1 absent an applicable existing one. Ordinary lawful elemental status buildup becomes **100% of delivered eligible elemental Power**, without universal ×0.70 on the attack. Preserve Blue Flame **50%**, White Flame **0%**, Wakecraft dedicated Freeze Attack **200%**, and other established exceptions; elemental status thresholds (0.5×/1×/4× and named exceptions), normal contact, direct damage, duration and costs remain unchanged. **The 0.65 MR weight and 0.70 defensive divisor are now approved**; historical 'typed SR unapproved' and elemental-END²-only notices are superseded. Distinct non-elemental status formulas and the Water-erosion SR-recalculation timing remain outside this approval. Full text: `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md` and `10_Canon_Changelog.md`.

## November 20, 2026 — THREE-FLAME PYROMANCY APPROVED (LATEST)

**Ordinary Fire:** 0% armor-MR bypass / full Burning; **Blue Flame:** 25% armor-MR bypass / half Burning; **White Flame:** 50% armor-MR bypass / no Burning or refresh. Ordinary spell costs/Power and independent protections are unchanged. Adaptive form selection supported. Author-accepted matchup design is roughly **85% Pyromancer wins vs Boughsteel**, **10% vs Nullporcelain** (with attainable weak-point counterplay), and around **40% vs Glassweave counter-builds**; these are goals, not guaranteed outcomes. Earlier Blue/White numerical 'experimental' notices in the index are **superseded**. Full canon and observed conditional simulation fixtures: `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md` and `10_Canon_Changelog.md`.

Approved: An attack encompassing and capable of damaging an entire castle may wound the castle as **one structure**; an attack reaching only a door wounds **only the door**. Coverage does not bypass resistance. Planned calibration: level **1/10/25/50/75/100** Pyromancers, at least half of allocation into INT+END, ten-minute rituals, mundane door versus **Boughsteel-reference unenchanted castle**. Structure/ritual numerical mechanics and Combat Lab support are pending. See `10_Canon_Changelog.md`.

Author direction: **most non-fragile items/structures use the existing wound framework**, leveraging nonlinear severity rather than a separate universal HP system. A comparably powerful martial attack can breach ordinary castle stone if lawful Power and physical resistance allow; magical rituals provide a distinct investment/scale advantage. Desired unenchanted castle progression: very-low caster no masonry breach; lower-mid ritual person-sized; mid ritual large; high caster one-spell breach and castle-destroying ritual. ## November 20, 2026 — ENCOURAGE INT² FORMULA APPROVED (LATEST)

**Encourage** is a nonmagical emotional-status reduction action: **two actions reduce buildup by INT²; one action by INT²/2**, without spellcasting implement modifiers or MP. Requires a strong personal relationship, spoken encouragement heard by the target, and **30-foot range**. Applies universal 4×/1× active-condition downgrade/end thresholds. This formula is approved canon, not merely provisional; the future Dispel Potion remains a design note. See `10_Canon_Changelog.md` and `15_Naga_Psycraft_Canon.md`.

## November 20, 2026 — UNIVERSAL ACTIVE STATUS REDUCTION APPROVED

Status reduction applies to **active conditions**, including extended/permanent ones: reduction below **4× resistance** drops Severity II to Severity I; below **1×** ends the condition. This is universal for all legal status-reduction mechanisms (including Wakecraft Cleanse and potential Encourage/Dispel Potion where they qualify). Status duration does not prevent downgrading or removal. See `10_Canon_Changelog.md` and `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`.

**Future potion (unpriced/unimplemented):** A Dispel Potion intended to reduce **all magical status buildup**. **Encourage direction:** Nonmagical spoken support from a close friend within **30 feet**, provided the victim hears them, can reduce mental/emotional magical status without mana or spellcasting implement bonuses; strangers or incidental allies do not qualify. **Provisional numeric proposal:** **INT²** buildup reduction for **two actions**, **half for one action**, pending approval/calibration. Mechanism for ending already-active or permanent mental control remains open. See `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `15_Naga_Psycraft_Canon.md`, and `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`.

## November 20, 2026 — FINISHER INTERRUPTION, CHARM REFRESH, STATUS DURATION APPROVED (LATEST)

A physical Finisher is interrupted if target eligibility ends before resolution or the attack cannot be completed (e.g. moved out of 5-foot range or attacker disarmed). Both 1× and 4× Charm last **3 rounds**, refreshed by additional Charm status application. Every status may use the universal modular spell-duration option only if the **final triggering spell** legally pays its extra duration cost. Details and precedence in `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, `15_Naga_Psycraft_Canon.md`, `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`, and `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR CORRECTION: Mental status thresholds 1×/4×; Finisher requires strong Charm (LATEST)

**Approved correction, controlling over the November 20 mental-status entries below:** The five mental conditions (Confusion, Anger, Fear, Charm, Sleep) use **exactly two severities**, triggered at **1× buildup (Severity I)** and **4× buildup (Severity II)**, consistent with other statuses. The previous entries incorrectly describing **2× as Severity II**, or suggesting that **4× is merely an optional duration-overbuild benchmark**, are **SUPERSEDED**. No extra intermediate severity is created. The attacker may release buildup on the same attack that applies it, or continue buildup toward 4×. Severity I/II effects and durations are those explicitly specified in the preceding approved status record: Confusion/Anger/Fear **1 round at 1×; 3 rounds at 4×**; Sleep **up to 3 rounds at 1×; 1 minute at 4×**; Charm **suggestibility and source-only attack penalty at 1×; active allegiance switch at 4×**. Charm and Sleep still halve applicable status-application magical Power. Extra spell duration investment and exact Charm durations remain unresolved.

**Finisher eligibility clarification:** **Severity I (1×) Charm does NOT make a creature eligible for a Finisher.** **Severity II (4×) Charm DOES.** Sleep and qualifying immobilization remain Finisher-eligible as previously approved. No additional automatic helplessness, instant death, or new Finisher defense bypass is implied. Older statements simply saying 'Charm makes eligible' are qualified by this ruling. All other previously approved Finisher rules are unchanged: two actions, attacker within 5 feet, ×4 damage, resolves at the start of the attacker's next turn and can be interrupted.

## November 20, 2026 — CLARIFICATION: Two status severities are distinct from 4× release examples (LATEST)

**Correction to the immediately preceding emotional-status entry:** The author's existing shared status system has **two severity thresholds: 1× buildup for Severity I and 2× buildup for Severity II**. However, the author's individual examples explicitly compare **1× release to 4× accumulated buildup**, including Confusion/Anger/Fear durations of **1 round at 1× and 3 rounds at 4×**, and Sleep of **up to 3 rounds at 1× and up to 1 minute at 4×**. **Do not reinterpret 4× as 2× or assert that releasing at 2× automatically gives the full 4× duration.** The details for duration when releasing between these named benchmarks, and how greater buildup affects the two severity profiles, remain OPEN. The author's Charm examples describe suggestibility at 1× and changing sides at 4×, even though the generic second-severity threshold is 2×; precisely reconcile Charm's advanced-status potency threshold with the universal two-severity mechanics before testing. All other attack targeting, breaking, subtle hypnosis, and Finisher decisions in the preceding entry remain approved as stated.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Mana potion catalogue, Toxicity implementation, extra refill doses and obsolete shop warning (LATEST)

**Approved offensive-price correction:** the provisional base offensive spell investment ladder now reads **0 MP cantrip; 1 MP basic; 2 MP enhanced; 4 MP strong; 8 MP greater; 12 MP superior; 20 MP maximum**, before area, duration or additional modifiers. The author-selected price benchmarks are retained as the **current WIP ladder**, not a verified final spell-Power-per-MP formula; the previous 11 MP superior and 18 MP maximum proposals are superseded.

**Approved Toxicity treatment:** rename Heal Burn to **Toxicity** throughout current usage. Toxicity can accrue from healing, mana restoration and other explicitly eligible effects. Using **maximum usable MP after Soul Strain** as the denominator, drinking a mana potion grants Toxicity equal to **100% × actual MP restored / current usable maximum MP**, with excess potion restoration wasted once MP is full. Toxicity is cumulative. At **100%** a recipient has a total of **1 Toxicity wound**, at **150%** total **2 wounds**, at **200%** total **3**, then one more total wound every additional 50 percentage points. Wounds are **threshold-based totals**, not added again each time a potion is consumed while already above a threshold; effects and wounds caused by reaching thresholds cannot be healed until rest permits it. Exact rest-based Toxicity reduction and release from wound-healing lock are still OPEN. Keep the preexisting per-injury healing-Toxicity percentage benchmarks pending a full healing cost review.

**Approved tiered mana-restoration potion proposal, now baseline for continued calibration:** One ordinary drink restores **Tier I (level ~10): 5 MP at 20 Gems; II (~25): 10 MP at 60 Gems; III (~50): 20 MP at 1,000 Gems; IV (~75): 40 MP at 8,000 Gems; V (~100): 80 MP at 25,000 Gems**. Effective potion restoration is capped at usable missing MP. The comparable **one-dose-per-day refillable** prices are **200 / 600 / 10,000 / 80,000 / 250,000 Gems**, respectively. At a given tier, each extra daily drink/charge on a refillable adds **50% of the tier's base refillable price**, repeatedly, with no numeric maximum count yet selected. Thus an item with N daily doses costs **base × [1 + 0.5 × (N−1)]**, before any as-yet-unapproved modifiers; each dose is a separate potion drinking/use opportunity. Replenishes **one use per daily charge**; additional purchased charges each also refill once per day. Refillable potion terminology, contents/vessel and interactions with rest or day boundaries remain to detail. The approximate 1:10 disposable-to-refillable price relationship and current economy references remain. Do not assume potions are manufactured or ubiquitous without crafting/availability rules.

**Approved use and threat:** Drawing a potion costs **one action**; drinking costs **one action**; drawing during a grapple needs a **Technique check** (numerical check and outcomes TBD). Eligible enemy prepared attacks or grapples can interrupt access or destroy the potion via ordinary legal interaction; no free reaction or extra attacks. Mana potions restore actual MP without changing maximum MP. Toxicity is shared with healing and future expressly eligible buffs, making replenishment trade off against injury treatment.

**Critical source-quality warning:** The old **'Consumables — Early Stock' Gem Shop inventory** in `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` (including old **20-Gem Mana Dew** and healing vial offers) is **OUTDATED / NOT A CURRENT PRICING REFERENCE**. It must be comprehensively revised before used for offers, economic comparisons, or future consumable balancing. Preserve historical records but never treat that catalogue as authoritative current market pricing. The present tiered potion reference and latest ≈878,286-Gem level-100 equipment portfolio, 30%-of-lifetime-earnings consumable allocation take priority.

**OPEN:** Healing-potion potency/toxicity pricing integration; short-rest / long-rest Toxicity clearing and treatment of threshold wounds; mana potion sourcing, alchemical craft requirements, inventory and cost materials; potion usability by zero-natural-MP Warlocks; crafting of permanent daily-refill vessels; refill trigger timing and stacking; precise MP-to-Power conversion; updated skill-specific spell costs; marginal price/value effect of MP cost-modifying implements.

---

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: 20-MP offensive benchmark and Toxicity resource-potion design (LATEST)

**Offensive spell investment, author-selected WIP design:** revise earlier **Greater** benchmark to **8 MP**, retain **Superior 11 MP**, and set **maximum unmodified base-effect/Power investment at 20 MP**, before duration, area, and other spell modifiers. Retain previously discussed basic 1, enhanced 2, strong 4, and 0-MP cantrip as **working ladder**, not a fully calibrated/approved MP-to-Power equation. Actual impact of additional mana on Spell Power and any overall price multiplier remain under testing.

**Rename:** The former **Heal Burn** mechanic is now **Toxicity**. Toxicity is a shared drawback accrued from relevant restorative effects, including healing and new mana-restoration potions, and potentially later potion buffs/other specified effects. This is a new balance subsystem rather than a blanket grant of potions/crafting ranks.

**Author-directed Toxicity thresholds:** accumulated Toxicity reaches harmful wound territory at approximately **100%**, with **1 wound at 100%, 2 wounds at 150% and +1 wound at each additional 50 percentage points** (200%→3, 250%→4, etc.). These Toxicity wounds **cannot be healed until the recipient rests**. The speaker also said 'once you exceed 100%'; the exact boundary wording is ambiguous and requires confirmation; **100% inclusive** is the working reading of the explicit 'at 100%, it's 1 wound' example. Existing Heal Burn's prior approved per-injury percentage schedule (superficial 5%, first ordinary injury 20%, first Guard 10%, next Guard 15%, etc.) is preserved **as a starting Toxicity accrual schedule**, pending a review of how healing and potions share the meter. Short rest/long rest Toxicity reduction amounts and how temporary wound locks lift remain to be integrated; do not invent cures or permanent recovery impairment.

**Soul Strain interaction:** for percentage-based Toxicity calculations, treat **maximum usable MP after Soul Strain** as the character's maximum mana, not the hypothetical unstrained natural reserve. Numerical mana restoration/Toxicity conversion is not yet approved. Avoid assuming Toxicity automatically lowers maximum MP, or that existing Soul Strain was removed.

**Mana potions and action economy:** Mana-restoring consumables are intended to restore actual spendable MP (up to the user's usable maximum). The user approves a tactical sequence requiring **one action to draw a potion and one action to drink it**; enemies can exploit legal prepared attacks on the potion or interfere with the user. **Drawing an item during a grapple requires a Technique check**, with its exact difficulty and opposed-resolution mechanics pending. This rule does not create additional attacks or waive reaction legality.

**Pricing direction:** propose tier-appropriate single-use mana potions priced at **roughly one-tenth of a full comparable item** of the tier, and **once-per-day replenishing/refillable potions** costing as much as the full comparable item. Use the **current** approved tenfold-redominated economic framework: level 10/25/50/75/100 primary item reference costs **60/120/240/480/960 Gems**, endgame wealth **3,200 Gems**, and consumables **30% of lifetime gross earnings (~2,014 Gems through level 100)**. Specific mana restoration values, tier potion prices, Toxicity-per-restored-MP equation, refillable daily charge count, exact potion availability and crafting are **PROPOSALS pending approval**. The older 20-Gem 'Mana Dew' shop offer requires reconciliation, not silent deletion.

**Other unresolved design issues:** immunity to healing above the Toxicity limit, how lasting/rest-locked wounds interact with preexisting injury states, blood/liver toxicity remedies, Nymbrin potion manufacturing and recipes, action interruption, whether unused healing/potion strength creates Toxicity, and handling of Warlocks' zero natural MP/external reserves.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR DECISION: Craft Mastery points and universal pool; offensive MP ladder under review (LATEST)

**Approved Mastery gain:** Each magical Craft has **20 Craft-specific upgrades** and **20 non-specific/universal upgrades** in the intended modular progression. Each purchased Craft-specific upgrade grants **0.5 Mastery points for that Craft**; each purchased non-specific/universal upgrade grants **0.25 Universal Mastery points**, applying to relevant Crafts. **Round down the final combined Mastery** (not individual fractional gains). Full investment grants **10 craft-specific + 5 universal = 15 Mastery maximum** for a Craft. Expressed as `Mastery(c) = floor(0.5 × craft_specific_upgrades(c) + 0.25 × universal_upgrades)`. Universal Mastery is one shared pool and is not repeatedly counted for the same Craft.

**Channel Limit integration:** Previous author direction sets the INT-derived component at **half its former weight, with the former −10 INT offset removed**, plus the separate automatically accumulated Craft Mastery. A natural **working formula, still awaiting numeric calibration/explicit ratification**, is `CL(c) = floor(effective_INT/10 + Mastery(c))`. The Mastery accrual and cap are approved; the combined formula's precise rounding, minimum CL, edge cases and calibration are NOT yet final. Do not revert to the legacy `ceil(max(1, INT−10)/5)` formula without recognizing supersession direction.

**Offensive pricing review, NOT approved:** The author recalls a possible ~18 MP maximum for unmodified base spell Power and is considering an MP-cost multiplier and future mana potions. Current GitHub records do **not** establish an approved general 18 MP offensive price ladder. Candidate comparison ladder: cantrip 0, ordinary basic attack 1, scalable stronger single-target attacks **2 / 4 / 7 / 11 / 18 MP**, excluding area, duration and specialized modifiers. These are **assistant proposals only**, not final canon. Exact MP-to-Power function, whether 18 is a ceiling or a reference investment, combat balance, high-level casting, and any future mana potion mechanics or availability remain OPEN. Do not invent an approved potion.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR DECISION: Unified Channel Limit, Warlock migration, interrupted rituals and compounded entropy (LATEST)

**Approved channel design:**
- Remove the historical **−10 effective-INT offset** from the future modular **Channel Limit** equation. The INT portion now matters **half as much as before**; a natural starting mathematical expression is **effective INT / 10** rather than the legacy adjusted **ceil(max(1, effective INT − 10) / 5)**. **Craft-specific automatic Mastery supplies the other half of the intended resulting Channel Limit**, earned through that Craft's learned magical disciplines rather than a separately purchased Mastery skill. The numeric rank-to-Mastery schedule, rounding, baseline reference caster and how a literal 50/50 split varies across differently invested characters are **not yet approved**; do not accidentally erase Mastery's independence by setting it equal to INT-derived points for all characters. Previous Channel Limit math remains legacy reference pending complete migration.
- The normal one-action maximum is **half the applicable full Channel Limit**, with a **minimum of 1 MP when casting an eligible mana-costing spell** even if ordinary floor rounding would yield zero; two-action spells can use their full applicable limit. **Cantrips may cost 0 MP** and still obey normal legality and **at most one offensive attack per turn**. Zero MP does not mean a second attack.
- **Warlocks are to be integrated into the universal Channel Limit framework.** Their legacy **END-based one-hand Handling Capacity cap** was designed before the wider spell system and is to be **replaced**, not retained as an independent competing ceiling in the modular migration. This does not silently eliminate their author-approved **zero natural MP**, requirement for actual external mana sources, handling timing, magical eligibility, or resource/access restrictions; exact revised Warlock holding/transfer/casting calculations and relationship to the old safe 30-second/absolute 60-second holding window are pending.
- In **cooperative rituals**, the **ritual leader alone determines applicable spell skill prerequisites, ability ranks and Channel Limit**. Other participants add real mana, subject to the established **50% entropy loss on each ally's contributions**; they do not independently increase the spell's Channel Limit merely by joining, and there is **no participant-count cap**. The precise per-time-step throughput of all added contributions and the status of lead-caster mana as distinct from ally-contributed mana still require numeric specification.

**Approved interruption rule:** A ritual **interrupted before completion fails entirely**. A partially completed working **cannot be immediately discharged**, reduced to a smaller spell, or salvaged as a half-strength attack. What specifically counts as an interruption, and whether invested MP is unrecoverably consumed rather than returned, remain **OPEN** unless independently settled.

**Approved compounded entropy:** A **cooperative one-week ritual applies both losses sequentially**. Each ally's contributed mana first suffers **50% cooperative entropy**, then the ritual's resulting total investment (including contributions otherwise eligible) suffers **another 50% week-long entropy**. Thus **100 MP supplied by an ally yields 25 MP of effective ritual investment** before other legitimate costs. The lead caster's own contribution does not automatically suffer the ally-only contribution penalty but does incur the week-long penalty. Permanent-spell Soul Strain remains **entirely on the ritual leader**.

**Open:** Exact Craft Mastery rank formula, racial effects, rounding, cantrip capacity handling if nominal CL is zero, specialization exceptions, one-/two-action allocation under formal ritual timelines, multiple casters' contribution throughput, Warlock revised handling, partial ritual MP recovery, and interruption adjudication. Preserve existing approved discipline prerequisites until formally migrated.

---

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Warlock shield and barrier interactions (LATEST)

**Status and precedence:** The author's latest explicit November 19 rulings supersede older suggestions of mandatory omnidirectional basic Warlock shields or a depleting shield-HP pool. These are approved qualitative foundations for the modular Manacraft migration; exact structure-damage procedures, arc, and outstanding spell Power formula remain open. Preserve the existing historical Warlock skill/rank/SP ledger until separately migrated.

- **Creature-anchored White shield:** A normal basic magical overshield is **directional, anchored to its target, and follows the target's torso orientation**. Knocking its bearer prone rotates its protected arc along with that torso, potentially leaving ordinary attackers an opening. It remains protective on **each eligible attack** during its duration, not a one-hit resource pool. Its normal defensive presence does **not** prevent allied attacks from leaving or entering, and allies' attacks are ignored by the shield.
- **Fast versus slow passage:** The shield is an obstacle to **fast, harmful strikes**: a rapidly swung sword meets resistance and can rebound. **Slow deliberate penetration** is physically possible: a careful foe may slowly reach a hand or sword through the field, but that slow poke does **not** become an injurious strike merely by touching flesh. Legitimate approach, bodily passage, positional control and grappling can bring attackers inside the shield; ordinary basic anchored shields **do not protect against close-range attacks within a grapple**. This does not allow arbitrarily relabeling a fast attack as a slow one; determine lawful attack effects using the ordinary combat and positioning rules. A trip or other maneuver must itself be legally delivered; the shield is not automatically bypassed because a maneuver is called a trip.
- **One target, one anchor shield:** An individual target may bear **one ordinary anchored shield** at a time. Multiple separately cast directional shields remain permissible across distinct eligible targets, with actual action, MP, duration, upkeep, and Channel Limit restrictions; do not invent a generic prohibition on multiple shield castings.
- **Timing and Power, author-selected baseline:** A basic anchored White Mana shield applies **100% of its eligible spell Power for the current turn**, against each attack within its protected arc; the **three-round** version applies **50% of eligible spell Power** against each such attack during that duration. The analogous basic **Red imbuement** adds its independently calculated spell-Power rider to **all eligible attacks** over the same one-turn 100% or three-round 50% options, never multiplies an entire physical attack. No extra attacks are generated. Existing action economy and Channel Limit still govern the casting. Larger-area and specialist Concordance improvements are not established by this entry.
- **Black counter:** An eligible Black Mana attack contacting a magical shield **subtracts its own applicable Power from the shield's remaining protective Power**, possibly unraveling it entirely. The reduced protection **stays reduced for the remainder of that casting**, rather than refilling each round. Black's previously author-selected wound-exploitation bonus is **+50% of its own base spell Power per existing bodily wound, capped at +100%**; that bodily-wound bonus does not automatically apply to a shield, which has no bodily wounds. Global additive-rider philosophy remains: independently calculated mana/spell effects do not multiply the completed combined attack.
- **Barrier wall (White and Green structure interface):** Unlike permeable creature-anchored shields, a **world-anchored structural barrier** stops physical travel from either side, blocks allied as well as hostile attacks, and is **probably transparent** (transparency is a design proposal, not locked). White walls and Green-created physical structures should share the future common structure-building/structure-damage system. **A physical attack that overcomes structural barrier Power can physically break the barrier**; it is not indestructible or exclusively vulnerable to countermagic. The user considers **two or three structural wound states**, but explicitly defers the number, thresholds, and actual break sequence until structure-creation mechanics are designed. Black reduction to magical barrier Power remains relevant; exact interaction with a partially broken structure remains open.
- **Open for calibration:** Exact directional arc (90° versus 180° was suggested but not approved); facing/turning and slow-infiltration timing checks; whether a shield's Power subtracts before or after other defenses; the detailed multiple-hit shield contest; base spell Power, MP and Channel costs; barrier geometry, transparency, structure wounds, and relationship between Green-created physical matter and White walls; Concordance's specialist superior protection. Do not use any earlier illustration or experimental calculator as final canon.

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Spiritus projection injury mechanics and full Warlock calibration objective (LATEST)

**Soul-Shaping / astral projection:** Abandoned body is **helpless**. **Cognum and Anima travel**; **Spiritus stretches from the body, tears off a portion and replaces it through significant upfront mana expenditure**, rather than fully leaving. Short-range incorporeal projection has a **visible tether** giving host/body mana access; tether has **1 wound**, defends using spirit's **END**, and **non-blunt attacks against tether face +1 difficulty**. **Cut tether → immediate return and 1 bodily wound**. **Destroy projected spirit (short or long) → return and 3 bodily wounds**; long-range return occurs **after approximately one minute**. Long-range spirit has no host-mana access through tether, suffers **approximately 50% penalty to all attributes**, but Warlocks **still may shape accessible external mana** without gaining natural MP. Detailed casting/return/range, new Soul Strain, healing, upfront MP, round-off, existing-wound and spirit defense interaction remain OPEN. Author approves completing Warlock universal and Craft-specific modular design systematically; historical five-skill ledger is preserved until expressly migrated. See [Warlock Manacraft](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR DIRECTION: Flexible Manashifting, Soul-Shaping boundaries, efficient Leywell filling (LATEST)

**Base Manashifting, proposed modular identity:** Red energizes existing targets/sources (and may speed creatures rather than automatically hurt), Blue temporarily morphs **shape not chemistry** and grants only circumstance-responsive, not arbitrary selected biological adaptations, Green **grows/heals/repairs** organisms **and objects**, including mana-created stone whose density/purity/strength improves from porous novice output to excellent masterwork with investment; White organizes physical clutter, improves clarity/accuracy/light and grants finite **all-angle overshields** rather than automatically infinite END stacking; Black degrades/exhausts, suppresses light, and may punish existing wounds more than Red. All effects remain conditional on resource, targets, resistance and future object-creation calibration.

**Craft directions:** Mana-Shaping expands targets/expressions and later blends colors; Spell-Shaping uses **actively shaped/invested modifications** to spells (no passive universal skill sharing); Soul-Shaping includes compatible-color **Anima mana transfer into a recipient's natural MP pool** (**affinity changing specifically rejected**), Cognum memories, Corpus sculpting, Spiritus tethered astral projection and possible whole-soul work. Short-range **visible astral tether** supports host mana access; long range loses that connection and suffers substantial attribute penalties. Warlocks still have **zero innate MP**, so projected Warlock mana logistics remain open. Full soul creation/reform/destruction is **deferred** pending Summoners, Cogmarshals and other systems. **Esotery exists within every Craft but each Craft's Esotery is distinct and not transferable**. Universal Transformation can extend lawful Craft changes for hours/permanently, with significant possible Soul Strain and costs, not free access to other Crafts.

**Mana economy:** Existing willing allies/spells/Leywells are accessible lawful sources; Medium unlocks further sources. Author approves **100% efficient transfers into compatible color-matched ordinary Leywells**, with no lost mana on transfer but all physical supply, capacity, single-color and handling limits intact. **Old Warlock skill ledgers remain until explicit migration.** See [Warlock Manacraft](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Magic and the Soul](01_World_and_Shimmer.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Warlock Spirit Anchors and Pattern Weaving refinements (LATEST)

**Pattern Weaving** holds intercepted spell pattern **together with its original mana in one hand**; the Warlock can retain that pattern or instead discharge the intercepted energy back as **raw mana of its original color**. A container loses the pattern. **Dispel** medium-seizure interpretation reaffirmed. **Primordial Mana** may be stored in **new specialized Leywells** (ordinary single-color Leywells are unchanged). A ghost/Phantom requires a cherished-in-life **Spirit Anchor**; destroying it permanently releases the ghost. Mana-starved ghosts lie **dormant inside their anchor until it is touched**, siphoning mana to remanifest. Ghostly memories are frequently incomplete and emotional; Phantoms remain independent and potentially hostile. Numerical formulas, leywell construction, spell-hand timing, and ghost manifestation/drain thresholds are deferred to the modular-spellcasting pass. **No new prices or formulas have been approved.** See [Warlock Manacraft](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Warlock Esotery five-rank direction (LATEST)

**I Ghost Mana** senses object-borne emotional traces and at higher Power fragments of words, and can summon exceptional tragedy-bound Cognum ghosts; hostile **Phantoms** are independent, mana-feeding, unfinished-business-driven and never automatically loyal. **II Pattern Weaving** drains and holds a spell's pattern in one hand, allowing recreation with the original modifiers and scalable mana investment no lower than the spell's casting minimum; containers destroy the held pattern. **III Dispel** seizes an incoming spell's medium with **twice ordinary absorption's disruptive Power**, dispersing its mana rather than harvesting it; **four channeling actions**, or **two plus one self-inflicted wound**. **IV Mana Conduit** alters magical reactivity and physical resilience of matter, including potentially reversing Glassweave's strength/weakness, and allows strong reciprocal END-versus-maximum-mana adjustments to creatures. **V Primordial Mana** combines all five mana colors into **colorless Wild Magic**, with **twice ordinary output potency**, a random manifested color, and possible bizarre phenomena including small Celestial World portals; intended preparation/storage in Leywells requires an explicit exception to ordinary one-color-only Leywell rules. **Numerical spell parameters and new mana storage construction are open**. Prior Warlock five-skill canon is preserved pending formal migration.

**Same-session Pyromancy correction:** Higher **Breath of Flame Power** changes oxygen **concentration/depletion in a small region**, not Range; modular Range is separate. See [Warlock Manacraft](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Pyromancy Fire Esotery I–V and Brimstone volatility (LATEST)

**Author-approved qualitative Fire Esotery design (November 19, 2026):** Rank I **Breath of Flame** manipulates and relocates oxygen, including extraction from water. Oxygen-rich pockets amplify fire Power and permit immediate flame spread; oxygen-poor pockets suppress/extinguish fire and increase MP costs of spells cast within because of physical strain. Stronger oxygen-rich zones can become latent explosive hazards and can slightly *reduce* spell costs through improved physical ability. Removing breathable air causes an immediate suffocation hazard for exposed creatures. Oxygen is a distinct, manipulable **Breath of Flame medium only**, not an additional source of fire/smoke medium and not a means of fueling other Pyromancy spells; the caster spends mana to reposition/control oxygen. Combining Breath of Flame with appropriate **Transformation** can create or remove oxygen within a living body, requiring the normal Transformation contact, resistance, costs and other applicable requirements; external oxygen manipulation alone is not unrestricted internal body manipulation. The precise enrichment/depletion thresholds, affected spell costs, amplification, explosive triggers, volumes, durations, atmospheric behavior, suffocation procedure and defense/resistance remain **OPEN**; no free area execute or unopposed instant death is implied.

Rank II **Ghostfire** permits magical flame to traverse selected **nonliving materials** without setting them alight or damaging them, while still burning creatures (including allies) and other unprotected matter; example: attack creatures on a rope bridge without burning the bridge. Selection scope, contact effects and spell pricing remain open. Rank III **Brimstone** uses real ash as mandatory medium, made solid through heat and mana. All Brimstone is inherently **volatile**, with **no caster control over degree of volatility**. It is exceptionally durable/stable **only on the turn created**; afterward it is explosively unstable, requiring a triggering impact/attack or ignition/heat to detonate rather than automatically exploding. Blast calculations, storage, trigger specifics, environmental interactions and safety are open. Rank IV **Ashen Reconstruction** rebuilds from ash: **still-hot ash** carries an intact memory permitting perfect reconstruction; once cold, its memory degrades and reconstruction is crude. **Phoenix Reversal** is the pinnacle pre-destruction application, cast beforehand on an object or person so that later reconstruction is possible; it is not a blanket free resurrection, and its cost, eligibility, timing, recovery, consent/resistance and relationship to death rules remain open. Rank V **Cursed Flame** targets a material named at casting, allowing that normally nonflammable substance to burn as though rich in fuel. Tougher materials, such as steel, burn longer rather than vanishing instantly; it requires normal oxygen and ongoing spell maintenance. Ordinary defenses and spell contests are not silently bypassed. These are approved *ability directions*, not a finalized numerical SP/casting ledger.

See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Wakecraft Esotery five-rank direction (LATEST)

The author selects the **proposed five ranks of Wakecraft Esotery**: **I Mirrorwake**, condensed to *water memories plus remote scrying*, with **meaningful ancient memories accessible even to a novice** where actually preserved; no compulsory age-based denial or vague-only result (clarity/reconstruction still context and Channel Limit dependent). **II Surface Dominion**, moldable **solid water approximately half as tough as comparable ice**; **III Refraction**, optical light distortion/projections/concealment through water; **IV Cloudcraft**, learned direct molding of clouds/fog/vapor **as solid**, clouds delivering **long-term effects with 2× mana efficiency**, and combined with Rank II enabling walking on clouds or fog and obstructing an enemy's movement in fog; **V Abyssal Pressure**, enabling extraordinary pressure through water irrespective of ordinary depth. These are chosen discipline identities/qualitative capabilities, not finalized MP, resistance, duration, structure-HP or reach formulas. **Cloudcraft is a specific advanced exception to the basic liquid-only Wakecraft active-control rule**; it does not retroactively grant unrestricted vapor control to all Wakecrafters. Original Mirrorwake five-rank document is preserved as historical approved material **pending formal SP/rank migration**; its old gating must not prevent meaningful novice historical readings under the newer explicit decision. See [Wakecraft](13_Wakecraft_The_Six_Arts_of_the_Living_Tide.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR CORRECTION: Hearthiron ×12 Medium reserve; Medium reach approved (LATEST)

**Hearthiron correction:** For proposed Pyromancy Banked Heat sourcing, **even a small quantity of Hearthiron counts as 12× its ordinary tile/space-equivalent thermal-medium reservoir**, **not ×3**. This is the user's current intended value; it may increase in a later approval. Ordinary Banked Heat from comparable substantial hot sources retains the separately directed **at-least-×3 heat-richness**. This correction changes **thermal-medium reserve equivalence only**, not existing Hearthiron armor Furnace Burst charge counts or equipment combat statistics.

**Approved Medium access:** active Medium extraction/control reach is **half the caster's normal attack spell Range**. The caster must **sense the eligible medium**, or **sense a piece continuously connected to the otherwise unseen eligible medium**. Connected fire and smoke may be accessed as a network **only inside the resulting control area**; mere continuity does **not** extend ordinary extraction beyond its radius. Under the proposed merged Magnitude attack ranges 30/90/300/1,000 ft/1/10 miles, the corresponding control radii are **15/45/150/500 ft/½/5 miles**. Source knowledge, extraction throughput, actual amounts, mana/Channel Limit, containment, release and specific Craft eligibility still matter. Extraordinary remote exceptions need their own learned rule and cannot be assumed. See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR DECISION: Medium revisions and thermal source richness (LATEST)

**Medium advancement design:** In the planned Wakecraft Medium progression, **swap the earlier suggested ranks III and IV**: III **Borrowed Moisture** (organics, vegetation, wood and potentially resisted living tissue), IV **Buried Spring** (aquifers, concealed reservoirs, saturated stone); I Dewcatcher, II Pure Current and V The Last Drop retain their discussed concepts. This is the approved direction for the **forthcoming universal Medium migration**; the existing independent Deepdraw rank ledger is preserved until formally replaced. **Pyromancy II Kindling is rejected**, replaced by **Ash Extraction**, allowing both **Hot Ash and Cold Ash** as media, with **ash material and heat extractable separately**. **Banked Heat (proposed Pyromancy III)** yields **at least 3× the comparable ordinary-fire thermal-medium quantity per tile**, and **even small Hearthiron quantities supply a reservoir of at least 3× ordinary size-equivalent thermal medium**; these statements refer to medium/heat density, **not automatic tripling of previously approved Hearthiron armor Furnace Burst charges**. Exact conversion units, heat-depletion accounting and ash medium eligibility by spell await definition.

**Proposed, not yet numerically approved:** Medium control/extraction radius = **half of the character's normal attack spell Range**. Under current proposed Magnitude range benchmarks this is **15 / 45 / 150 / 500 ft / ½ mile / 5 miles** at Rank 0/I/II/III/IV/V. Access requires the caster to **perceive a source, or a connected portion of eligible medium they can perceive**; continuous fire/smoke can disclose connected unseen sources within the control region, but harvesting remains limited by legitimate medium quantities, Channel Limit, mana, handling/release requirements and environmental connections. The half-range rule, perception definition, continuity through smoke/flame/water, and throughput are still under discussion, not final numeric canon. See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR CORRECTION: Hero/Legend Execute wound progression; SOUL color confirmed (LATEST)

The author explicitly distinguishes **Heroes and Legends** from ordinary Execute targets. **Ordinary targets:** Execute Power divisor ÷4 at 0 wounds, ÷3 at 1, ÷2 at 2, and ÷1 at 3 or more (floor ÷1; the user's agreement resolves the earlier division-by-zero issue). **Heroes and Legends:** automatically resist while below 3 wounds; at **3 wounds Execute Power is ÷3**, at **4 wounds ÷2**, and at **5 or more wounds ÷1** under the same decrement/floor progression. These values **supersede the older suggested ÷1 at three Hero/Legend wounds**. The user confirms Warlock Soul Reaping awards mana of the victim's **SOUL color**, not soil color. The general 10 MP Execute starting cost and all previously unresolved resistance formulas, specific Soul Reaping cost/yield, and lawful delivery requirements remain unchanged. See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Warlock Manacraft](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR DECISION: Execute spells, universal non-wound finishers (LATEST)

**Execute** is the universal classification for magical effects that, if not resisted, inflict **non-wound-based instant death or make the target a noncombatant for the rest of the scene**. **Base casting cost is 10 MP** and **base Execute Power is one-quarter (÷4) of the equivalent normal attack spell Power**. The author's wound-based direction says **each wound on the target reduces this divisor/multiplier by 1**: tentative direct reading is ÷4 / ÷3 / ÷2 / ÷1 at 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 wounds respectively, but **behavior at 4+ wounds (divisor reaches zero) needs explicit clarification, not automatic infinite Power**. **Heroes and Legends automatically resist an Execute while they have fewer than 3 wounds**, irrespective of otherwise eligible contact. A failed applicable resistance produces the fatal/scene-removal outcome; precise resistance/defense check and whether qualifying wounds refer to injury stages are open. Ordinary Craft medium/delivery/targeting, Channel Limit and action rules still apply. **Warlock Mana Harvest V Soul Reaping becomes a lethal Execute and awards a massive amount of mana of the victim soul's color (author typed 'soil'; interpretation requires confirmation), instead of XP damage**; numerical yield and how its prior 20 MP / upkeep applies await decision. Wakecraft's total bodily-water extraction is also an Execute candidate. Existing special soul-storage protections (Astral Hearts/Celestial Recall) are not silently repealed. See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Warlock Manacraft](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md), and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Scry and magical Perception use independent range rules (LATEST)

The author explicitly agrees with **separate range rules for Autonomy Rank IV Scry, including perception through a spell**, rather than automatically limiting remote sensory access to the caster's ordinary Magnitude/Range distance. Free inherent Craft-specific magical perception likewise should have its own appropriate reach/sensory requirements instead of simply inheriting generic casting distance. The precise numerical ranges, connection/anchor eligibility, remote-control restrictions, and what magical perception can detect are **not yet approved**. This does not grant unlimited remote surveillance or unrestricted remote casting. See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR DECISION: Autonomy five-rank structure (LATEST)

**Autonomy** governs how independently a magical effect behaves, **not how long it lasts**. Its five-rank design is **I Persistence** (a spell can continue without active control, but duration remains separately determined); **II Perception** (the spell perceives its surroundings as its caster can, including eligible magical perception; at this stage its sole autonomous instruction is to activate when it perceives a specified trigger); **III Reactivity** (behavior can respond to changing circumstances instead of merely a one-time trigger); **IV Scry** (caster can perceive through and remotely control an existing spell and possibly update its instructions); **V Intelligence** (grant independent intelligent decision-making by paying more mana, **without subtracting INT from the caster**). Retain distinction from **Magnitude** (how many spells can be sustained). **OPEN:** duration/upkeep costs, trigger specificity, rank III reaction repertoire, Scry range/visibility/command scope, eligible spell senses, and exact Rank V mana cost/limits. This is author-approved progression direction, not a finalized numeric spell catalogue. See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR CORRECTION: Transformation uses delivered media, not bodies as automatic media (LATEST)

**The prior 'bodies themselves serve as medium' description is superseded.** Transformation spells are **cast as normal spells**, but their effect must be **carried to/imbued into the living target via lawful Craft medium contact**. **Wakecraft:** target already soaked or struck by a qualifying water attack. **Pyromancy:** target already has the **Burning condition** or is struck by a new qualifying fire attack. **Psycraft:** requires the established focusing crystal/gem plus qualifying **Cipherlight contact**. Delivery makes a creature eligible for Transformation, **not automatically transformed**; usual casting costs, Channel Limit, action, targeting and resistance continue to apply. Discussed applications include Cipherlight conversion with gem imprisonment, temporary gaseous transformation, and ash disintegration. **Permanent transformations are intended to be among the most expensive and easiest-to-resist magic**, with numeric prices, resistance difficulty, containment, duration and reversibility unresolved. This latest correction controls over older wording. See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR DIRECTION: Transformation affects other targets; five-rank conceptual framework (LATEST)

**Transformation is not self-only:** its learned effects can be applied to other eligible creatures, including allies or hostile targets, subject to future targeting, resistance and resource rules. Transformation primarily serves supportive/body-modifying roles, but can also be used offensively. A practitioner may use a living body as a **qualifying medium for suitable learned Transformation workings**; this exception does not make every bodily substance freely accessible to unrelated Crafts or waive existing spell costs, medium/Channel Limit requirements, or magical opposition. Examples: a Pyromancer may transform an attacker's foot into fire, possibly causing a trip and supplying fire that can then spread, or alter allies to breathe smoke harmlessly. Actual trip adjudication, duration, targeting, and elemental spread remain open.

**Accepted design progression, not finalized spell ledger:** I **Control** (Pyro body heat, Wake water balance/sweat, Psy subconscious introspection); II **Hostility** (Pyro burning contact/body, Wake self-blood weaponization and clotting, Psy touch/voice/body-delivered Power); III **Adaptation** (survival traits **plus distinct spells increasing or decreasing physical resistance to the applicable element, and distinct spells increasing or decreasing magical resistance to it**); IV **Alteration/Projection** (hybrid bodily reshaping and projecting bodily functions/influence); V **Transmogrification** (partial/full elemental embodiment). Applying effects to other creatures is allowed in principle, but no unrestricted no-save hostile transformation is approved. Final ranks, MP costs, Channel Limit thresholds, SP costs, defense/typed-resistance arithmetic and medium/targeting details remain for calibration. See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR DIRECTION: Merge Range and Magnitude into one universal magical discipline (LATEST)

**Range and Magnitude are ONE progression discipline**, combining remote reach, area of effect, and multiplicity of sustained workings. The author accepts the numerical design benchmarks discussed: **base reach 30 ft; former Range ladder 90 ft / 300 ft / 1,000 ft / 1 mile / 10 miles**, **area up to 30 ft at low progression, 300 ft at the previous Rank III benchmark (10× 30 ft), and 2 miles at mastery**, with **two concurrently sustained spells at the lower multiplicity unlock and eight at the higher**, each intended to scale with automatic Craft Mastery. Spells may create an effect centered on the caster even without remote Range sufficient to place its origin elsewhere. Reach controls placement, area controls spread, not damage for free; mana, Channel Limit, accessible medium and concentration continue to matter. **Not yet formally finalized:** exact redistribution of these benchmarks across the five ranks of the merged discipline, Mastery scaling coefficients, area shapes, required MP/Channel Limits, and interaction with existing approved Wakecraft Longwake/other Craft rank ledgers. The merger does not authorize silently replacing existing ranked skills, prerequisites or SP costs. See [Shared magical mechanics](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 19, 2026 — AUTHOR DECISION: Automatic magical Craft Mastery and capacity-gated capabilities (LATEST)

**Magical disciplines and Channel Limit have separate jobs:** the caster must **learn the relevant discipline rank** to unlock a spell, modifier, or transformation, **and** have sufficient **applicable Channel Limit** to execute it. Learning an effect does not itself bypass its channel requirement. **Craft Mastery is automatically earned from advancing that Craft's magical disciplines**, rather than purchased as an independent Skill Point skill. The author directs that the **INT-derived component of Channel Limit be halved** and supplemented by **craft-specific automatic Mastery**. Exact formulas, rank-to-Mastery mapping, rounding, multicraft handling and treatment of established specialized Channel Limit exceptions are **OPEN**, so do not apply unapproved numeric conversions. Partial self-transformation (e.g. water-form hand extending an attack) may have a smaller channel requirement than full self-transformation, subject to the same prerequisite discipline. Existing one-action/two-action channel allocations remain in force until reconciled. Universal magical disciplines are not automatically granted to martial or item-crafting Crafts (e.g. Jinx). See [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — Ash terrain, Hot Ash / Cold Ash (LATEST AUTHOR DECISION)

**Extinguishing fire:** Each extinguished fire tile leaves **one tile of ash**. All ash is **difficult terrain**, costing **twice the normal movement** to traverse a tile. Ash less than **one minute old** is **Hot Ash** and applies **half the Burning status buildup of equivalent fire exposure**. At one minute or older it becomes **Cold Ash**, with no Hot Ash Burning effect. A Pyromancer may **draw heat from an ash tile as though from one smoke tile**, making that tile Cold Ash. Pyromancers are also intended to have a **spell to reheat ash**; its prerequisites, cost, action, duration, and exact mechanics remain open. Preserve established fire and smoke rules. See [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md) and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 19, 2026 — Wakecraft Cleanse utility (LATEST AUTHOR DECISION)

**Cleanse:** A Wakecrafter spends **1 MP and one available action** to reduce **their own status buildup of any kind** by an amount **equivalent to the status buildup their ordinary attack would apply with that action**. The user must actually be able to take an action; Cleanse is utility, not a damaging attack. **Approved timing clarification:** Cleanse can target **any positive status buildup (>0)** before the status has begun to affect the Wakecrafter mechanically; it does not require an activated penalty. This is not a free reaction or out-of-turn action. **Open:** one versus multiple buildup types per activation, whether reduction reverses the effects of an already-active condition, and exact attack-equivalent magnitude/defense calculation. [Wakecraft](13_Wakecraft_The_Six_Arts_of_the_Living_Tide.md); [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md); [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 19, 2026 — Wakecraft Water Attack, Ice Stab, Freeze Attack, and Shatter (LATEST AUTHOR CORRECTION)

**Three approved Wakecraft offensive modes:** **Water Attack** deals physical damage and builds Water erosion; **Ice Stab** deals physical damage and builds Freeze; **Freeze Attack** deals **zero direct damage**, is classified as **magical energy**, applies **double Freeze elemental status magnitude**, and **instantly converts existing Water elemental buildup into Freeze buildup**. **Shatter:** a physical attack powerful enough to inflict injury **before any Shatter bonus** doubles its **incoming Attack Power before defenses**, **not final damage**, and removes the target's Freeze. Unknown Freeze thresholds, conversion/erosion expiry interaction, and typed status defenses are not numerically approved. Owners: [Wakecraft](13_Wakecraft_The_Six_Arts_of_the_Living_Tide.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — Initiative uses Combat Speed, not effective SPD (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Author-approved supersession:** Initiative order compares each combatant's **current ordinary Combat Speed** (effective SPD + effective DEX/4, with approved armor, wound, and equipment-shortfall modifiers), **highest first**, rather than the former effective-SPD-only score. The existing tie priorities remain: Heroes/Legends before ordinary combatants, player characters before tied NPC Heroes/Legends, and DM adjudication of any remaining exact tie, fixed for the combat. Ordinary movement still uses SPD. Dash's temporary Combat Speed bonus does **not** retroactively reorder established initiative; recalculation timing after genuine mid-combat stat changes remains unresolved. Older statements in this index and its linked historical entries that say initiative uses effective SPD are **superseded for initiative only**. Detailed owner: [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md); approval: [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 19, 2026 — Water MR erosion thresholds confirmed (LATEST AUTHOR CORRECTION)

**Water Elemental Magnitude now has numerical MR erosion thresholds:** At **≥1× the target's applicable Water Status Resistance**, the Water condition reduces **applicable MR by 15%**; at **≥4×**, by **30% total**, not an additional 30%/45% overall. The prior “erosion amounts undecided” wording is superseded. Water does not inherit Fire's Burning injury schedule or ordinary half-SR PP; it erodes **Magic Resistance, not physical Armor Resistance**. Normal three-turn matching-element magnitude accumulation and refresh survive. **Open:** final typed SR + MR integration, erosion duration/recovery, percent rounding, and preventing self-feedback when eroded MR helps determine Water SR. References: [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Combat Endurance](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Wakecraft](13_Wakecraft_The_Six_Arts_of_the_Living_Tide.md), [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Pass 2 weapon enchantments and Guardian AUTHOR APPROVED

The author approved all **37 entries** in the Pass 2 shortlist. [Weapon Enchantments](36_Weapon_Enchantments.md) is the rules reference for the 36 generic weapon enchantments and Guardian for shields. It records slot ranks, eligibility, Mixed and Full values, triggers, and durations. **Achilles’ Heel is generic. Concussive spends one injury stage to remove one action, or two stages to Stun for one turn, with at least one injury stage remaining.** The older Mantis Blade entries that describe its values as pending are historical. Equipment and Leywell rules are in [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md).
