## November 18, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Implement ratings, seven properties and pooled two-implement resources (LATEST)

**Approved Implement Ratings, Quality I–V: 26/30/35/40/48**, with **independent equipment Power = IR² 676/900/1225/1600/2304**, added to appropriate caster attribute Power rather than multiplying it with F. The updated **seven tuning properties** are **Offense, Defense, Utility, Mana Efficiency, Reservoir, Reach, Soul Strain**; **Control was removed**. Offense/Defense/Utility steps each adjust the corresponding **implement portion** by **±25% per notch**. Mana Efficiency steps adjust actual MP expenditure by **∓20% per notch**, preserving nominal action/turn Channel Limit. Reach has **no approved numerical range/step** and should not be exploited for free downgrade points. Tuning-point policy remains 0 start, each real downgrade +1 (max two per property), +1/+2/+3 positive steps costing 1/3/9 total points.

**Reservoirs** remain finite and real, Quality I–V baseline **Medium Level I–V**, floor 0, ceiling V; replenish spent material. At neutral, implement Soul Strain is zero. Enchanted-item reference Soul Strain **S = 1/2/4/6/8** across Quality. Soul Strain attribute −1 incurs S, −2 incurs 2S; positive +1/+2/+3 grants additional usable reserve MP of S/2S/3S (as accepted in the author's subsequent approval), not extra natural END or Channel Limit. If two implements are used simultaneously, **their compatible medium reservoir content and their Soul Strain/bonus mana-capacity effects may be combined**, but **each spell must use ONE chosen implement** for rating, tuning, efficiency and focus effects. Ordinary hand, action, mana-color and Craft prerequisites remain.

**Open:** Spell MP-to-damage curve and medium-specific spell list, Reach baseline, mana rounding, psychic reservoir expression, formal numeric offensive spell defense formula and physical Weapon Rating replacement status. Owners: [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Naga Psycraft](15_Naga_Psycraft_Canon.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Smoke one-tile visibility and additive casting implements (LATEST AUTHOR CORRECTION)

**Smoke visibility supersession:** See and attack **through one 5-foot tile of smoke at one harder accuracy category**; **more than one tile along the sightline blocks line of sight**. Prior automatic additional categories for each tripling of smoke coverage, and the nine-step visibility threshold, are superseded. Existing correctly-guessed-tile **50/50 single-target blind attack** may still operate subject to lawful targeting; the full targeting interaction is pending. Pyromancers' oxygen-free smoke breathing remains.

**Implements must add independent Power** rather than apply legacy weapon-F multiplication to caster attributes. The intended baseline is **comparable to a Medium weapon across physical Quality I–V**. A proposed `A² + ImplementRating²` structure is for testing and the implement rating ladder is **not yet approved**. This additive implement correction does not itself retroactively ratify the experimental physical Weapon Rating replacement formula; leave that model's separate status intact. Seven-attribute tuning, one-hand minimum, two-implement investment and finite 0–V reservoirs remain as separately instructed. Owners: [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Smoke visibility and customizable implements (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**Smoke:** One smoke tile worsens attack accuracy by **one difficulty category**, and each **tripling** worsens accuracy by another category. Literal thresholds 1/3/9/27/81/243/729/2187/6561 correspond to smoke steps 1–9; past nine steps smoke completely blocks target sight. Guess the target tile; a correct guess gives a **50% chance** for a single-target attack to resolve normally. How smoke is spatially counted, d20 Impossible clamping versus sight and the post-guess accuracy interaction remain open. Ordinary non-Pyromancers usually need minutes of inhalation to suffer smoke problems, to be designed in an environmental Endurance catalogue; **Pyromancers can breathe oxygen-free pure smoke without issue**.

**Implement crafting:** Seven customizable implement attributes (specific categories/magnitudes **not yet finalized**) with crafting presets and a **zero-point starting budget**. Each valid downgrade (up to twice per attribute) yields **+1 tuning point**; upgrades cost **1 / 3 / 9 cumulative points** for **+1 / +2 / +3** steps. No free points from downgrades that have no real effect. All appropriate implements have **finite compatible-medium reservoirs**, baseline **Quality I→Medium Level I, II→II, III→III, IV→IV, V→V**, hard maximum V and minimum zero. Each reservoir downgrade lowers Medium Level by one, up to two real reductions; no points below zero. Spent medium requires replenishment. Ordinary implement handling occupies at least one hand; two can be carried at meaningful Gem/equipment opportunity cost, not automatically stacked or used for bonus actions. Psychic reservoir analog remains unresolved because Psycraft has mandatory gem/Cipherlight, not a Refiner. Exact seven attributes, unit bonuses, raw Power/Quality implementation, prices and presets are **NOT APPROVED** by this policy. Owners: [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Psycraft](15_Naga_Psycraft_Canon.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Revised threefold Medium Levels, Typhoon and contested spell Power (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Corrected medium ladder:** Each spell still needs both MP and eligible expendable medium. Wakecraft levels I–VII are **1 cup / 1 waterskin / 1 tile / 3 / 9 / 27 / 81 water tiles**; Pyromancy **match-sized flame / lantern flame / 1 tile / 3 / 9 / 27 / 81 fire tiles**. **One water tile equals three puddles** and spreads across three shallow puddle squares, replacing the old four. If a caster commits a larger medium source to a smaller spell, **one-third of the larger committed source is consumed**. Ordinary dispersed requirement is **two Medium Levels higher**: level III→V = **9**, IV→VI = **27**, V→VII = **81** dispersed tiles. Old 4/12/36/108 values are superseded. Four cloud tiles to one condensed full water tile is a separate existing conversion and not implicitly changed.

**Pyromancy correction:** **Fire↔smoke manipulation is basic, natural Pyromancy**, not exclusive to a learned smoke specialization. The size of a generated smokescreen depends on fire dispersed; exact active tile conversion remains OPEN. Each fire tile still generates one smoke tile per round. **Typhoon:** all creatures suffer **1 PP and one harder accuracy category**; Wakecraft treats **each rain-filled Typhoon square as one puddle**, so three qualify as one full-tile equivalent, naturally renewed while Typhoon rain persists. Ordinary light rain remains dispersed medium.

**Contest and hazards:** A defending caster trying to seize an opposed spell/medium makes an **Average Naked-d20 check (8+, 65%) at equal effective spell Power**. At **2×, 4×, 12×** relative Power advantage shift **one, two, three categories** easier; corresponding disadvantage shifts harder, clamped to existing six d20 categories. **Multiple seizing defenders' Power adds**, as does multiple outgoing casters' Power; defensive casting implement contributes to defensive Power. Guaranteed deterministic equal/double-Power seizures are superseded by chance, while full-spell versus medium seizure conditions/actions need reconciliation. Fire spread carries fractional **25% tile** growth; a creature adjacent to newly spreading fire is ignited on **d4=1** (25%) and faces basic nonscaling environmental fire damage (numbers OPEN).

**Open:** spell Power/skill/implement arithmetic, sub-tile and beyond-VII dispersed rules, full-spell takeover procedure, precise medium/fire conversion, Refiner capacities/refills, typhoon stacking, fire exposure and damage. Owners: [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Wakecraft](13_Wakecraft_The_Six_Arts_of_the_Living_Tide.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Seven Medium Levels and dispersed environmental casting (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**Medium Levels:** Spells have **both an MP cost and an expended/committed physical Medium Level**. Levels **I–VII** for Wakecraft are **1 cup water / 1 waterskin / 1 water tile / 4 / 12 / 36 / 108 water tiles**; for Pyromancy **match flame / lantern flame / 1 fire tile / 4 / 12 / 36 / 108 fire tiles**. The consumed/expended medium may be dispersed by use, survive physically or be recovered where legally possible. **Dispersed media** (appropriate smoke, rain, widespread shallow flooding and cloud) demand **two higher Medium Levels' worth of dispersed tiles** than concentrated casting (III→V=12, IV→VI=36, V→VII=108); Level I–II conversions and Levels VIII–IX are uncalibrated. Each burning tile produces **one smoke tile/round**; rain tiles renew each turn while raining; smoke techniques remain separately learned.

**Wakecraft environmental transformations:** Cantrip-level gathering may **melt existing ice** or **condense 4 cloud tiles into 1 tile's quantity of usable liquid water**, without creating water or overriding liquid-only active control / weather restrictions. **One tile's water quantity can spread as a puddle over four squares**, without quadrupling its substance; further water expands flooding. **Pyromantic fire spreads at round start by 25% of existing burning tiles**, where compatible fuel exists, downwind outdoors or randomly as DM adjudicates indoors. Adjacent creatures roll a **d4** against ignition, for a basic, usually non-scaling fire hazard. Exact d4 threshold, fractional spread rounding, smoke ventilation, area geometry and interaction with established elemental conditions remain OPEN.

**Contested media and implements:** Media must be within legal range and not already committed to another active spell unless seized through a contest. Author wants the **defensive casting-implement bonus** involved in an opposed medium-seizure check; reconcile this with the previously approved **effective-Power** thresholds before approving a new roll procedure. Finite Refiner reservoirs hold genuine concentrated media rather than discounting Medium Level; capacities/refills still open. Psychic gem/Cipherlight magic does not inherit this tile ladder. Owning references: [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Wakecraft](13_Wakecraft_The_Six_Arts_of_the_Living_Tide.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Finite Refiner reservoirs AUTHOR APPROVED (LATEST IMPLEMENT DIRECTION)

**Approved:** A **Refiner casting implement** contains a **specialized Leywell with a large but finite reserve of real, compatible physical medium**. A Blue-attuned Refiner may hold **liquid water** for Wakecraft; a Red-attuned one may hold **genuine flame** for Pyromancy, respecting all existing Craft media rules. This is a **finite, depletable and refillable** medium reservoir, **not** automatic endless generation, MP-to-matter conversion or a percentage reduction in spell medium requirements. Medium capacity and replenishment mechanics remain OPEN, and stored mana is distinct from the stored physical medium. Ordinary Leywells do not automatically gain this storage capability. **Psychics do not have a Refiner**; their alternative implement profile is undecided, and their gem-focus requirement remains intact. No proposed numerical specialization modifiers are approved. Owners: [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Psycraft](15_Naga_Psycraft_Canon.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Spellcasting power philosophy, implements and per-action channeling (LATEST APPROVED)

**Latest author-approved spellcasting rule:** The combat turn remains **two actions, maximum one offensive attack action**. A **one-action cast** may channel at most **floor(full applicable Channel Limit / 2)**; a **two-action cast** may channel up to the **full Channel Limit**. This refines the previous shared turn-scale Channel Limit/prepared defense framework without granting extra MP or separate offensive attacks. Preserve existing specialized Channel Limit exceptions. Two-action preparation/interruption/charging edge cases are still open.

**Spellcasting and martial parity direction:** The four contributors to combat performance are **base attribute, weapon or Craft-specific casting implement, enchantment and Craft**. Implements increase affiliated spell potency, normally **optional**; **Psychic/Psycraft requires focusing gems** as a deliberate weakness. Pyromantic lanterns are another illustrated implement. Offensive and defensive cantrips must remain available at any level as weak fallbacks; offensive cantrips are weaker than comparable weapon attacks, while basic mana-costing attacks (e.g. Firebolt-style) remain effective versus same-level opponents even at level 100. Further mana can increase damage but may require larger/consumable media, dangerously broaden area or charge over turns. **Psychic favors concentrated single-target output, Pyromancy grows area with high output, Wakecraft is intermediate.**

**Latest elemental-vs-physical spell correction:** Direct energy effects receive their eligible **elemental status / Elemental Magnitude** advantages and **no additional blanket raw damage multiplier** for being energy; indirect magically propelled physical attacks face appropriate physical defenses. **No exact spell Power/MP, implement, area, charge or cantrip formula is approved.** Owners: [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Sorcery](03_Sorcery.md), [Psycraft](15_Naga_Psycraft_Canon.md), [Wakecraft](13_Wakecraft_The_Six_Arts_of_the_Living_Tide.md), [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Full is 2× Mixed magical effect at equal enchantment slots (LATEST APPROVED)

**LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL:** At identical invested **1–6** enchantment slots, **Full** mode provides **twice Mixed's eligible numerical magical effect** in return for **no permanent stat enhancement**. **Mixed** retains its eligible smaller permanent attribute bonus plus its normal effect; **Blank** is attribute-only. **No bonus Full slot**, no Quality-based enchantment effect multiplier, no doubling of mundane weapon Attack Power or new attack action. The approved ordinary elemental **Mixed** per-contact Power by slots 1–6 is **430/1,350/3,800/5,800/8,100/15,000**, and Full is **860/2,700/7,600/11,600/16,200/30,000**. This supersedes earlier references to Full being merely one *Quality-based* effect tier above Mixed. Mantis Blade's specific bonuses and mode calibration, Blank/Mixed attribute-to-slot tables, categorical-effect scaling, Leywell/MP logistics, and typed SR formula remain unresolved. [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Hypothetical Sharpness enchantment power benchmark AUTHOR APPROVED (LATEST)

**APPROVED DESIGN BENCHMARK ONLY, NOT AN AVAILABLE ENCHANTMENT OR NEW DAMAGE RULE:** For comparing proposed enchantments and future spells, use an always-active hypothetical Sharpness effect of **+5% final physical Attack Power per invested enchantment slot**, with 1–6 slots = **+5% / +10% / +15% / +20% / +25% / +30% total**. Apply once to completed physical Attack Power, not to STR/DEX or F before squaring. Example at five slots: `Power_sharpness = 1.25 × Power_mundane`. This does not establish any actual spell damage/MP/SP price or remove conditional activation and action-economy tradeoffs.

**Precision and precedence:** The author selected a simple approximate comparison ruler, **not** guaranteed 50/50 win rates at every slot. Earlier provisional element-only win-rate modeling suggested nearer **+40% at six slots** than +30%; that difference is retained as an uncertainty to retest, not a revision of the approved simplified benchmark. Previous studies used a NOT-APPROVED typed Status Resistance model `(effective END + 0.65 × MR)^2`, so actual parity may change. Enchantment strength depends on its **1–6 occupied slots** rather than host equipment Quality. Mundane physical Quality still affects normal weapon and armor calculations separately. Hypothetical Sharpness is **never a purchasable enchantment**; Mantis Blade percentage progression and Blank/Mixed/Full-to-slot resource remapping remain unresolved. Existing approved elemental weapon per-contact Power 430/1,350/3,800/5,800/8,100/15,000 and elemental statuses are unchanged.

## November 18, 2026 — Enchantment slot-only scaling supersedes Quality effect tiers (LATEST AUTHOR CORRECTION)

**LATEST APPROVED PRECEDENCE:** Enchantments themselves have **no Quality** and receive **no separate effect-strength boost from physical item Quality**. Their potency scales with **1–6 invested enchantment slots**. The approved elemental weapon Power table for all eligible elemental families, including Burning/Ember Edge, now maps **slots 1/2/3/4/5/6 → 430/1,350/3,800/5,800/8,100/15,000** delivered Power per valid contact. Mantis Blade / Mantis Strike also uses **1–6 slots**, but its exact percentage bonuses are **NOT APPROVED**. Older references to Quality I–V Mixed effects and QV Full Tier VI as determinants of enchantment Power are superseded, not additional bonuses. Mundane item Quality still governs physical equipment independently. **OPEN:** Slot eligibility, Blank/Mixed/Full, Leywell and mana economy remapping; do not invent replacements. Owning references: [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Stronger unified elemental weapon Power ladder AUTHOR APPROVED (LATEST)

**Latest explicit approval:** All eligible elemental weapon enchantments share the now-approved delivered Elemental Power ladder **Tier I 430 / Tier II 1,350 / Tier III 3,800 / Tier IV 5,800 / Tier V 8,100 / Tier VI 15,000** per lawful elemental contact, where the Tier VI effect is permitted on eligible Full-enchanted Quality V equipment. This **supersedes** the older Fire-only Tier I–V **226/554/1335/2456/3914** numbers and all below statements treating the stronger common power ladder as experimental or Tier VI output as unset. The separately proposed typed-Status-Resistance equation **`(effective END + 0.65 × MR)^2 × R_element` is NOT approved** by this numerical Power decision. Approved qualitative MR and element-specific resistance contributions to typed effective SR continue; their exact combined equation remains OPEN. Burning Edge's three-turn accumulation/refresh, thresholds, injuries and media delivery are unchanged. Owning references: [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Magical Media](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Equal elemental weapon Power I–VI and MR-aware thresholds (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**All eligible elemental weapon types have identical delivered Elemental Power at the same enchantment effect tier**, not reduced coefficients for particular elements. Current approved Tier I–V numeric baseline is **226/554/1335/2456/3914**, previously tested on Fire, until a unified new ladder is approved; Tier VI exists for eligible Quality V Full with Leywell VI but **Tier VI delivered Power is still open**. The author directs a recalibration where **MR0 is very susceptible**, Glassweave's moderate MR offers a small reduction in effectiveness relative to the former Fire benchmark, and high-MR Nullporcelain is a strong counter. **MR and specific Elemental Resistance increase typed effective Status Resistance** above approved base effective END²; exact combined equation not approved. Lightning Static has no passive consequence; discharge **below 1× effective SR has no effect**, valid ≥1× discharge applies 3 rounds Shocked (−1 action/turn) and 4× adds one-round Stunned and Trapped. Ice 1× slows SPD 15%/halves tiles, 4× freezes with magic or STR² struggles, 12× entombs without self-escape. Hex drains personal/equipment MP and at 4× ends ongoing magic, with exact amounts and injury cadence open. The suggested **430/1350/3800/5800/8100/15000** Power and `(END+0.65MR)^2×R_element` are **unapproved testing candidates**, not operative canon. Details: [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Water/Air elemental status exceptions (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**Water** erodes **Magic Resistance** in counterpart to Acid's AR erosion. **Air** from 0.5× Status Resistance adds **half its magnitude** to other elements; above 1× SR doubles other elemental magnitudes for effects; at 4× SR expends **all elemental magnitude** in a damaging **quadruple-power explosion** that clears all elemental conditions. Neither Water nor Air inherits the ordinary 0.5× SR self-Pressure Point. Existing **SR = effective END²**, three-turn same-element accumulation/refresh, real-contact delivery and approved Fire injury cadence remain in force. Exact MR erosion and Air explosion accounting remain unapproved. See [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Elemental Magnitude framework mirrored beyond fire (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**All eligible established elemental status types** now share Fire's legal-delivery, separate cumulative Elemental Magnitude, three-turn status with every valid matching-element hit **adding magnitude and refreshing the timer**, and **0.5× / >1× / 4× Status Resistance** severity bands (trace/no inherent effect; one ongoing self-PP; substantive effect; intensified effect). **Fire alone has the current mapped Burning-damage and doubled-damage outcomes**. Ice/cold, lightning/shock, water, air, toxic/hex and acid/corrosion remain **pending element-by-element outcome approval**. Preserve acid's approved AR loss (minimum 0), lightning's conditional metal-conduction advantage, and genuine pathways/material defenses. Full distinctions and unresolved decisions: [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md); equipment application: [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md); approvals history: [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Status Resistance, Burning Edge and fire injury cadence (LATEST APPROVED)

**Status Resistance (SR) = (effective END)²**, including the full value of eligible equipment END and the usual effective-attribute modifiers. Elemental Magnitude accumulates actual elemental Power in a three-turn status. Burning Edge Fire Power per eligible contact is **226 / 554 / 1,335 / 2,456 / 3,914** for Tiers I–V. Each such contact adds Fire Magnitude and refreshes the fire timer. Fire from half SR causes one ongoing self-Pressure Point. Above SR, Burning deals **one injury stage every second round**. From four times SR, Engulfed deals **two stages each round**. Apply the usual injury and Shimmer Guard progression. Physical or magical interventions can cleanse or worsen the condition. Water can put out fire, oil can intensify it, and conductive metal can strengthen lightning where there is a genuine path. Tier VI Fire Power, the exact tick and expiry boundaries, and refresh rules for other elements remain open. Owners include [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), and [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Contact Factor retirement and historical fire defense

**Historical fire defense:** **SPD was the ignition chance defense; END plus specific Fire Resistance determine the intensity/injury of fire once ignited.** The real flame persists until extinguished/fuel exhausted, with **no repeat SPD/END stat rolls** to determine burn duration/intensity each round. **Smoldering** after resisted initial damaging heat imposes **one meaningful self-PP** and a **modest Fire Resistance decrease** (exact amount still OPEN). Lower SPD means easier ignition, **not an approved automatic burn-duration multiplier**.

**Contact Factor is REMOVED** as a universal numerical elemental multiplier; retain lawful delivery pathways, ordinary insulation and distinct elemental protections without duplicative factors. **Lightning only** is the author's specific intended candidate for a conductive-metal-armor advantage; exact numerical bonus/conditions undecided. Ordinary Burning Edge I–V targets peer-relative parity; Tier VI Mana Blade exceptional. See [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Modular Draft](24_Modular_Equipment_Design_Options_DRAFT.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Burning Edge correction: SPD ignition and Smoldering (HISTORICAL)

**Latest author correction:** SPD determines **chance to ignite upon actual legal fire contact**, **not post-ignition burn duration**. Once lit, real fire continues until extinguished/deprived of fuel, with **no per-round stat tests** for duration. If initial fire damage is resisted but smoldering takes hold, **Smoldering** imposes one meaningful self-applied PP and a modest Fire Resistance reduction (exact percentage pending, assistant's −10% not canon). Burning Edge I–V must preserve **approximately equal relative effectiveness against peer-level Heroes**, even as magnitude scales to their stronger defenses; a small level gap may favor higher-tier weapons. Tier VI Mana Blade is exceptional. Older SPD-duration tables, progressing peer-level status severity and numeric ignition/Contact Factor multipliers are unapproved; contact transmission through physical material remains important. See [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), and [Modular Equipment Draft](24_Modular_Equipment_Design_Options_DRAFT.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Burning Edge naming and historical SPD-resisted afterburn

**Burning Edge** is the author-approved common name for fire-element weapon enchantments, not separate names by tier. Ordinary tiers I–V apply the existing real-medium conditional-contact elemental rider without multiplying physical Weapon Rating or character attribute Power. Tier VI is the already-approved **Mana Blade**, a wholly fiery blade absent when unpowered. The **ongoing burn is Speed-resisted (effective SPD), not END-resisted**; initial ignition remains a distinct Elemental Magnitude/fire-protection interaction. Numeric magnitude/duration/injury mechanics are **not yet approved**. See [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Tier VI 60-MP Leywell, Mana Blade and equipping times (LATEST APPROVED)

**Full enchantments have a Leywell one tier higher than comparable Mixed items.** Mixed QI–V = 10/20/30/40/50 MP, Full QI–V = **20/30/40/50/60 MP**, including approved **Leywell Tier VI (60 MP)**. Tier VI Minor/Standard/Powerful activation costs are **4/8/24 MP**; the Tier VI **Mana Blade** is an entirely fire-made blade that disappears without power, and its Powerful five-round activation costs **24 MP** for **two uses per full well**. Whole-element weapon transformation moves from older Rank V to **Tier VI**, with actual fire delivery/Elemental Magnitude mechanics outstanding. Physical item Quality VI is not created.

**Interactions:** One combat action can **remove and replace a ring/similar small accessory**; armor **donning Light 2 actions / Medium 2 minutes / Heavy 10 minutes**. Doffing timing remains open. See [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Enchantment resources, Full Tier VI and item actions APPROVED (LATEST)

**Enchantment resources:** Blank and Mixed **stat bonuses are constant while legally equipped and paid for through Soul Strain**, not periodic Leywell MP; **active magical enchantment effects incur NO Soul Strain** and spend appropriately colored Leywell MP. Mixed retains a moderate stat bonus plus normal effect; Full retains **no stats**, grants **one effect tier higher**, and pays that higher tier's **ordinary** MP cost. QV Full can use a **Tier VI effect**, approved in concept as **one simple peak-output effect comparable in base numbers to a moderate specialist**, with **few activations** from the highest established **50-MP** Leywell; Tier VI numeric cost/effects remain open. The previously tentative one-tier principle is now approved. See [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

**Item interactions:** One action to retrieve/take accessible objects or operate ordinary environmental objects (e.g. doors); **one action to draw or sheathe up to two accessible weapons**. A single ready thrown weapon from a bandolier/quiver can be **drawn and thrown in one action** with a free hand or a legally freed hand, subject to the turn's single offensive action limit. Exchanging equipped rings costs genuine item interaction actions and does not increase the **two** active ring positions. Precise two-ring replacement sequence remains unresolved. See [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Blank, Mixed and Full enchantment modes (AUTHOR DIRECTION)

Three item-enchantment modes: **Blank** = stat enhancement only (Quality I–V reference **+6/+12/+20/+28/+36**); **Mixed** = moderate stats (**+3/+6/+10/+14/+18**) plus ordinary effect tier matching item Quality; **Full** = **no stats**, more powerful effect one tier higher than Mixed. **Potential effect Tier VI** on Quality V Full-enchanted items is a **design direction, not a finalized numeric effect catalogue**, and does NOT add physical Quality VI or Leywell Tier VI. The item-mode scheme is established; exact effect cost, mode stacking/slot eligibility, Tier VI implementation and stat-upkeep mechanics remain unresolved. See [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 18, 2026 — Secretly prepared active enchanted defense (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Active defensive enchantments follow the existing prepared defensive-action model (Parry and applicable prepared magical defenses), not an on-demand free reaction.** On the defender's own turn, **secretly commit the required one or two combat actions**, identify the specific equipped defensive enchantment, and declare a **narrow, legal, outcome-independent trigger** in advance. The prepared enchantment takes effect at its lawful response opportunity if that trigger occurs and all requirements (including appropriate Leywell power, physical medium where relevant, and ordinary positioning/eligibility) hold. The defender cannot wait to see whether the attack would hit or deal damage before choosing the trigger or defense.

**Examples:** “The next attack against this named ally” or “the next attack made by this specified enemy” may qualify when the item actually supports that coverage. “The next attack that hits me” is not eligible as an outcome-dependent trigger. Follow the already approved preparation/expiry and relevant action limitations of the defensive framework being used; no additional defensive action, free attack, or reserved action beyond the existing two-action economy is created. Magical defense mechanics are not automatically granted the Parry-specific +1/+2 hit category, Pressure Point rewards, cooldown, or shield routing unless the individual enchantment explicitly earns such behavior under its actual approved rules.

**Distinction preserved:** Action-free passive modifications of an existing defense (e.g., an eligible preactivated reinforcement effect) remain passive; an enchantment that **independently performs an active defensive maneuver** consumes the preparatory action(s) and obeys the secret-condition system. The five-round duration of ordinary magical enhancements is distinct from the time window of a prepared, triggered active defense. Further item-specific duration on activation, expenditure timing if a trigger never occurs, trigger expiry, response scheduling, multiple prepared effects and coordination with the single prepared spell constraint require explicit calibration. They are **not** authorization for unlimited simultaneous active defensive reactions.

## November 18, 2026 — Passive versus active enchantment action economy (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Classification by behavior, not simply by whether magical energy is spent:** An enchantment that merely **modifies a legal action or enables a new capability** is a *passive/modifying activation* and **costs zero combat actions to switch on**, even when it consumes Leywell MP and maintains a time-limited magical effect. Examples include igniting a sword, boosting movement speed, or enabling an alternate mode of movement without actually moving the user. A character may activate **as many eligible passive/modifying enchantments as desired during their own turn**, subject to actual installed items, sufficient correctly colored Leywell mana, necessary real media, ordinary eligibility and any separately approved restrictions; no universal one-passive-per-turn cap.

**Independent actions:** An enchanted item that **actually performs an action the user would normally spend actions on**, including moving the wielder, launching its own attack, or actively defending, requires **one or two combat actions** according to the effect's design. These active uses must respect the existing **two-action turn and maximum one offensive attack action**; a magically produced attack is not an extra free strike merely because an item creates it. The exact 1-versus-2 action assignment remains for individual enchantments.

**Durations:** Previously approved ordinary enchantment activation lasts **five combat rounds** unless an explicit item rule provides an exception. Action-free activation does **not** mean MP-free activation. Independent actions and sustained modifying effects still consume their relevant Leywell MP according to applicable costs.

**Timing and interruption questions OPEN:** Resolve whether an **ordinary spell in the act of casting or channeling can be interrupted by damage**, and if so which damage events qualify, whether a check is required, what happens to paid MP and partially completed actions, and which separately approved exceptions survive. **Use that spellcasting decision as a reference before deciding whether damage similarly interrupts the existing two-action Leywell-recharge operation.** Do not assume all damage automatically cancels charging, or that it can never interrupt charging. Active enchanted defenses also require future integration with prepared defensive spell timing, turn windows and existing reaction/action constraints. None of these unresolved questions alters existing specific Shocked or anti-magic interruption effects.

## November 18, 2026 — Leywell charging action, rounding, and five-round enchantment activation (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Transfer accounting:** A legitimate contributor spends their own MP of the Leywell's exact matching color. Apply the approved **90% transfer efficiency** and **round the amount actually deposited DOWN to whole MP** after calculating the transferred amount; never round each constituent point up. Thus 10 MP spent → 9 stored, while 5 MP spent → 4 stored. Charging cannot exceed the Leywell's maximum (Tier I–V capacities **10/20/30/40/50 MP**) or the contributor's available MP. Fractional transfer yield is lost, not retained as partial MP. The contributor bears the actual spent MP cost, even when the destination fills before all theoretical yield would fit; efficient contributors may choose the amount transferred.

**In-combat recharge timing:** Charging a Leywell takes **two combat actions (approximately six seconds of real-world time)**. This is an action cost for a charging operation, not two additional mana charges or an automatic mana-generation process. The amount actually chargeable in one operation is bounded by available compatible MP, 90% efficiency rounded down, and remaining Leywell capacity; no additional fixed per-action transfer cap is approved here. The physical access, interruption and out-of-combat procedural details remain open.

**Ordinary enchantment activation:** **Most enchanted-item effects activate without spending a combat action**, and each normal activation lasts **five combat rounds**. The effect uses its Leywell for its applicable activation MP cost. An enchantment's explicit exception can have different timing only where separately approved; activation is not a free independent offensive attack or a license to exceed established action/effect limitations. Do not infer unlimited stacking or a free extra strike. Whether repeated activation refreshes the duration, and exact simultaneous activation limits, remain open.

**Approved working enchantment activation-cost ladder (user accepted preceding proposal):**

| Leywell tier | Minor effect MP | Standard effect MP | Powerful effect MP |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| I | 1 | 2 | 4 |
| II | 1 | 3 | 5 |
| III | 2 | 4 | 7 |
| IV | 2 | 5 | 10 |
| V | 3 | 6 | 12 |

These are general **initial design bands**, not an automatic cost or power assignment to every effect; individual enchantments still need compatible color, accessible medium where relevant, and actual effect design/testing. Previous assistant-suggested ten-round duration is superseded by five rounds. Existing exceptional material equipment effects (e.g. Skyvein's approved 6 MP per powered turn) remain under their owning specific rules unless expressly revised.

## November 18, 2026 — Leywell direct-MP recharge, fixed efficiency and five-tier capacity (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Latest author correction supersedes earlier devotional recharge:** Charging a Leywell **spends the contributing character's own actual MP**. It is **not** an independent mana-generating prayer process, does not create free MP, and does not depend on sincerity, faith, or devotional enthusiasm. Transfer efficiency is a flat **90% for every contributor**, independent of belief: a transfer of 10 MP from a legal source deposits 9 MP, with 1 MP lost. The precise accounting of fractional MP deposits and partial transfers remains open, so no further rounding rule is inferred.

**Strict color identity:** Each Leywell is bound to **one mana color**. It **only accepts transferred mana of exactly that matching color**; e.g., a blue Leywell cannot accept red mana. It does not transmute colors. The contributor must actually have spendable mana of the required color; an affinity alone is not free mana.

**Approved Leywell capacity by tier:** Tier **I = 10 MP, II = 20 MP, III = 30 MP, IV = 40 MP, V = 50 MP**, maximum stored amount. These are Leywell tiers, not automatic character-level grants. No Leywell may be charged above capacity. The former proposed 4/8/16/28/40 reservoir capacities, prior speculative 8/16/32/48/64 ladder, END-linked prayer charging and 10%-efficient reluctant-prayer rule are superseded.

**Duration-first enchantments approved:** Most installed enchantments should operate **for a defined duration after activation**, rather than requiring a new mana payment per hit or individual contact. Their magical power still comes from their appropriate Leywell and their outward effects still require real physical media where applicable. Exact duration, upkeep, per-effect MP cost, simultaneous effect handling, and activation action rules remain to be approved.

**Mundane media maintenance direction:** Elemental enchantments using ordinary fuel/water should consume **extremely small** physical quantities, with practical refueling after roughly **one day of heavy combat or a week of lighter skirmishes**. Fuel is common and easy to replenish (e.g., lantern oil or water). Its physical presence and sabotage vulnerability remain meaningful, but it is the mana supply that does most of the work. The cadence is the author's approved design direction, not finalized per-activation volume, a universal clock or new purchased supply tariff.

**Not yet approved:** Assistant-proposed minor/standard/powerful Tier I–V activation-cost ladder **1/1/2/2/3**, **2/3/4/5/6**, **4/5/7/10/12** MP respectively; tentative sustained-combat duration of up to ten turns; exact transfer-rounding rules; charging time and any charging action constraints. Do not treat these numbers as canon.

## November 18, 2026 — Enchantment philosophy, real media, and Glassweave Leywells (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Author-approved foundation:** Ordinary enchantments are restrained, purpose-built magical engineering, **not** copies of sophisticated racial Craft spells. Enchanted equipment uses existing magical principles and physical media to execute limited, repeatable functions; it does not grant a wearer mastery of Pyromancy, Wakecraft, or another racial Craft, nor reproduce an elaborate craft technique (for example, a Wakecrafter's independently controlled water-dragon attack). Ordinary enchantments may inject mana into real eligible media to cause basic effects, in a manner broadly comparable to the limited medium-directed application of mana by Warlocks; this comparison does **not** grant Warlock powers or bypass other Crafts' prerequisites.

**Intrinsic enchantments and Anvilcraft:** Anvilcraft's established magical material-working and alteration furnish enchantment's distinctive intrinsic expression. A smith may further alter an item's own properties by enchantment along lines consistent with what Anvilcraft already accomplishes, such as additional magical lightening of a Trufula weapon, without gaining unrelated racial Craft abilities. The scope, magnitude, costs, and precise eligibility of individual intrinsic enchantments remain to be designed; do not infer unrestricted matter transformation.

**Real-medium rule:** Spectacular outward effects must honor genuine medium requirements. An enchanted flaming weapon is not an eternally burning immaterial spell-flame. The author's approved **flaming sword engineering concept** uses (1) a very thin, minimal coating of stored flammable oil dispensed by the weapon, (2) ignition through a tiny **Hearthiron wick**, and (3) mana supplied by an activated **Leywell** to make the established flame burn substantially hotter. The weapon needs **both physical fuel/medium and mana** for its enhanced flaming effect. Its fuel reservoir is a legitimate sabotage or depletion vulnerability; the Leywell is considerably more resilient. This is an approved qualitative construction concept, **not** finalized oil consumption, reservoir capacity, ignition frequency, elemental magnitude, MP draw, or wound mechanics. Other outward enchantments likewise cannot invent required media by merely relabeling their effect as intrinsic.

**Leywell physical form (approved clarification):** Leywells are **not little vials of liquid mana**. They are usually polished **Glassweave orbs or Glassweave cut gemstones**, whose glow visibly communicates the power held within. Their stored magical energy is not a liquid. Preserve existing alternate names *well* and *faithwell*, and already approved capacity/activation rules where separately defined. 'Usually' does not forbid rare exceptional designs; no new universal toughness, price, charging rate, or storage capacity is established here.

**Power source and implements:** Ordinary enchantments **always draw their operating magical power from Leywells**; do not assume they can directly burn the wearer's natural mana reserves or work solely because the wearer has the matching mana color. Future **casting implements** may have narrowly specified exceptions after spell design is settled, but they are specialized **Craft-specific** tools, not universal enchantments usable by anyone with the correct mana color. Do not finalize such exceptions now.

**Preservation and scope:** Established magical colors, medium dependencies, Anvilcraft/Smithcraft prerequisites, physical material effects, Soul Strain rules, and prior approved Leywell-powered mechanisms remain in force. Prior speculative rune/gem-slot counts, active enchantment MP costs, named enchantment families, and catalogue quotas remain **unapproved** until separately authorized. This entry records philosophy and the flaming-weapon example, not a fully calibrated enchantment catalogue.

## November 16, 2026 — Reach distances and Technique-based movement control APPROVED (LATEST)

**Approved:** Short weapon attacks **1 tile (5 ft)**; Medium attacks **1 tile normally or 2 tiles at +1 harder hit-difficulty category for lunging**; Long attacks **1–2 tiles normally**. **Contested movement uses Technique, not the obsolete SPD ratio**. Short and Medium weapons contest the **1-tile** zone only; Long weapons contest **2 tiles** only, expressly **not adjacent at 1 tile**. A credible defender can resist departure/passage across its zone; Long weapons can resist a close-in through their 2-tile barrier with Technique. A successful contest allows lawful movement, failure stops at the boundary without free damage. At most one strongest-controller contest for a particular crossing, with other credible defenders handled under existing Pressure principles. **No automatic or no-action grapple; no routine opportunity attack.** Grapple initiation remains its own committed offensive Technique check. Exact contest-to-d20 category/Pressure values and repeated crossing timing remain open. Existing 1.25/1.50 SPD entry gates are superseded. See [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Melee Catalogue](25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md), and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 16, 2026 — Dash movement-tile multipliers and upward rounding APPROVED (LATEST)

**Approved for the two-action combat pilot:** **Dash / Half Dash (1 action)** grants **+50% movement tiles**, **Double Dash / Full Dash (2 actions)** grants **+100% movement tiles**; use the already determined ordinary tile allowance and **round any fractional result UP**: ceil(M × 1.5) or ceil(M × 2). Do **not** recalculate the SPD-to-tiles progression from temporary Dash Combat Speed. Existing **+25%/+50% Combat Speed** bonuses remain separate and unchanged. This **supersedes** the November 15 "does not multiply movement" pilot text. **Ordinary base tile allowance and Speed thresholds remain unresolved**; illustrative 5 tiles or stat thresholds are NOT canonized. See [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

---

## November 16, 2026 — Grapple initiation ONE Technique check APPROVED (PROCEDURE STILL ACTIVE; SCORE SUPERSEDED NOV22)

**Approved and operative:** Initiating an eligible grapple requires **one Technique d20 check** using the **current `max(effective STR, effective DEX)` statistic** (superseding the November 16 historical `2 × effective STR + effective DEX`), **not** the older ordinary attack-accuracy check or two consecutive rolls. Actual opportunity to reach the target and establish contact remains subject to approved physical/reach-control rules. Initiation is an offensive maneuver limited by the turn's single offensive action, not a free hit or automatic damage. Prior notes calling Technique initiation only a proposal are **superseded**. Exact opposing Technique-to-d20 category mapping, 1-versus-2 action cost, repeat attempt limit and Pressure shifts/duration are open. [Combat reference](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md); [changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). Base movement-tile calibration remains open; the later Dash-specific movement multipliers are documented in the latest entry above.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Technique = 2STR + DEX; Pressure hinders grapple escape (HISTORICAL SCORE; SUPERSEDED NOV22)

**Author-approved generalization:** **Technique = 2 × effective STR + effective DEX**, replacing the narrower label Grapple Control without changing its approved weighting. Non-damaging combat-control families include grapple, trip, disarm, shove and throw, but procedures for the latter four are deferred. **Net Pressure on the grappling escaper must make escape harder.** The proposed existing-tier +0/+1/+2 harder categories for net 0/1–2/3+ PP are **under review**, not numerically approved. The earlier **grapple initiation requires an attack check** ruling remains operative; replacing it with a single Technique check after real reach-entry opportunity is **the assistant's unapproved recommendation**, awaiting the author's choice. See [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). No equipment or damage formula changed.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Grapple Control `2STR + DEX` (HISTORICAL; SUPERSEDED NOV22)

**Approved:** Grapple-specific control attribute **`G = 2 × effective STR + effective DEX`** for holder and escaper; Strength counts twice as much as Dexterity. Supersedes equal-weight `STR+DEX` proposal. See [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). **Still under test:** exact `G_holder/G_escaper` to Naked-d20 six-category escape mapping, escape/initiation action costs, failed-escape PP expiry and stacking. Existing approved reach-in-grapple rules, shield interception bypass, required initiation attack roll and failed-escape immobility/+1 PP remain intact. No additive WR authorization.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Grapple-range interaction direction approved; escape roll calibration OPEN (LATEST)

**Author's latest grapple rulings:** A true grapple prevents ordinary Long-reach attacks against the grappler, makes eligible Medium-reach attacks **one d20 difficulty category harder**, permits legal Short-range/unarmed and physically possible one-handed ranged attacks, and prevents ordinary two-handed ranged attacks within that grapple. A grappler's attacks against their held opponent bypass **normal shield Guard interception**, not worn armor or END. Grapple initiation requires an attack check; a turn-based escape attempt uses a **STR/DEX-favoring d20 check**, with failure preventing ordinary departure from the square and causing **one incoming PP**. Exact escape formula, action cost and PP duration are **not yet approved**. The `STR+DEX` ratio mapped to the existing six-category Naked d20 chart is a proposal only. [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) owns the detail, with approval boundaries in [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). Proposed additive Weapon Ratings remain experimental.

---

## November 16, 2026 — END-maximizing offense-nonviable builds OUT of core balance tests (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**Approved testing scope:** In future weapon, armor, Quality, and general combat calibration, **exclude extreme END-focused builds unable to wound plausible more-generalist opponents from core headline results and mandatory balance objectives**; such highly durable but offensively useless or ineffective builds are allowed, not errors requiring special equipment compensation. Test normal generalists, viable defensive hybrids and representative armor/weapon progression instead; extreme END fixtures may appear only as optional, clearly separated, non-binding stress diagnostics. The exclusion is based on actual offensive viability, not merely investing meaningfully in END. Earlier END-specialist-first calibration objectives are superseded as testing priorities, while historical data remain historical. [Owning melee calibration notes](26_Melee_Power_Calibration_DRAFT.md) and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). **No numerical weapon/armor or injury rules changed; additive WR remains experimental.**

## November 16, 2026 — Power Attack self-Pressure and single-target identity APPROVED (LATEST)

**Latest operative Power Attack:** [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) now uses **2 actions, one single-target physical attack, ×1.25 final Attack Power, and ONE self-applied incoming Pressure Point until the start of the user's next turn**. It **does not impose a Power Attack-specific hit-difficulty penalty**. Its one target cannot become an ordinary Sweep/Wide Sweep/Line multi-target attack. Current ordinary Full Attack and Quick Attack/Double Strike action limits remain. A **genuinely accessible protected head gap** remains **+2 harder difficulty, actual AR25, ×1.25 final Power**; combined with Power Attack it is still **×1.5625 final Power**, but **+2 categories only** from the head gap, plus one self-PP on the attacker. Genuine exposed head uses existing +1/AR0 without bonus. The prior accuracy-based Power Attack version is expressly superseded. [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md) records author approval and live status; no Weapon Rating or other equipment damage formula has been replaced.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Headshot precision damage APPROVED; Power Attack self-Pressure alternative UNDER REVIEW

**Approved headshot refinement:** [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) now specifies a **genuinely accessible vulnerable protected helmet/head gap** as **single-target, +2 hit-difficulty categories, actual AR25, ×1.25 otherwise final physical Power on lawful contact**. A **genuinely exposed head** is **+1 difficulty/AR0 with no extra headshot Power**; a fully covered head without an actual gap gains no bonus or free gap. A legally combined **Power Attack + protected-gap headshot** currently applies **three harder hit-difficulty categories total** and **×1.5625 Power** if connected. **Power Attack is STILL 2 actions, +1 harder category, ×1.25 Power.** Replacing its accuracy penalty with **one self-applied incoming Pressure Point is an unapproved alternative**, not an enacted change. [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md) records the explicit approval and open decision.

## November 16, 2026 — Power Attack +25% APPROVED; headshot Power under study

**Approved new offensive maneuver:** [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) now owns **Power Attack**: one **2-action Full Attack**, **+1 hit-difficulty category**, **×1.25 of otherwise final Attack Power** after the usual weapon calculation and before ordinary END/covered-AR Impact. No extra attack, no stacking another attack action, no automatic defense bypass, and no Quick Attack/Double Strike combination on the same turn. **+50% Power Attack is not approved.** Additional **headshot Power bonuses remain exploratory**; retain the existing genuine exposed-location/armor-gap targeting rules with no automatically exposed head. See [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md) for approval and unresolved precision study.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Independent Weapon Rating / meaningful Quality design principles APPROVED; NUMBERS UNCHANGED

**Latest author decision:** Future weapon-system design should consider **individually assigned independent Weapon Ratings**, retain a **lasting offensive identity for every weapon design** rather than using Light/Medium/Heavy weight alone as damage, and make **higher equipment Quality genuinely meaningful without extreme wound-severity jumps**. These three **qualitative principles are approved**; neither `A² + WR²`, `Fₚ × A² + WR²`, the 31 draft rating ladders, nor any numerical replacement is approved. **Existing `Power=(A × F)²` stays the live physical pilot rule.** Overall STR/hybrid/DEX balance continues to account for previously accepted **armor and versatility tradeoffs**; raw one-hit Slashing hierarchy alone is not a holistic balancing verdict. **Indefinite quality scaling is not the present focus**, and prior speculative beyond-V notes remain nonoperative. See [Melee Weapon Catalogue](25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md), [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md) and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 15, 2026 — Shield Deflect stance RETIRED (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Latest explicit author correction:** **Deflect is removed from the playable Mystaria combat rules, including the current physical tabletop pilot.** It is vestigial and is **not** an available shield stance, action, reaction, alternative Guard mode, or shield-specific one-Pressure-Point counteraction. A shieldbearer may use legal **Guard**, **ordinary shield bash**, or **Light/Medium shield-and-weapon Double Strike** (Heavy shield Double Strike remains prohibited), plus other independently approved actions and prepared **Parry** under their actual rules. **Ordinary offhand Defense with a genuinely usable offhand weapon remains intact** and is *not* renamed, deleted, or granted for free merely by holding a shield. All older passages offering or approving shield **Deflect** in this document or other canon files are **historical and superseded**; historical descriptions are preserved solely to show design evolution. Nothing else about Shield Rating, physical contact routing, durability, magical Guard, Pressure, Double Strike, Parry, Dash or movement is changed. Future balancing questions no longer include whether to keep Deflect.

---

## November 15, 2026 — Two-action physical combat PILOT APPROVED; player-led balancing questions parked

**Latest author ruling:** Play the reviewed combat package with friends naturally, at the author's pace; **no AI-run playtests, scheduled testing or required follow-ups**. **Approved pilot**: two actions/max one offensive attack action per turn; Full Attack and Full-only Double Strike (2 actions, existing 60% final Power per hit); Quick Attack (1 action, **full Power, one harder category**); Naked d20 **Certain automatic / Easy 4+ / Average 8+ / Hard 12+ / Very Hard 17+ / Impossible no roll**; wider defender/attacker Combat Speed ratio cutoffs **0.20/0.80/1.25/2.50/5.00**; physical shield **Candidate A** Light/Medium **+1**, Heavy **+2** harder difficulty on defendable lines with one-die **miss/shield/body** routing; Half/Full Parry **1/2 actions, +1/+2 category, 1/2 earned PP on genuine prevention**; Half/Full Dash **1/2 actions, +25%/+50% Combat Speed**.

The new pilot routing supersedes automatic physical Guard and overlapping older shield obstruction **during pilot play**, but preserves existing lawful equipment, break overflow, magic interception and material rules; **Deflect was subsequently retired by explicit author correction**, and is not available even during the pilot. No final permanent-balance claim. [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) owns all pilot procedures and the **future human-play balancing question list**; [Physical Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md) details physical shield routing; [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md) records approval and supersession.

## November 15, 2026 — Vesperyn race name and naming format APPROVED

**Vesperyn** (singular and plural) is the approved race name replacing the working term *Lambfolk* for the Scarlands sheepfolk pyromancer concept. Their approved cultural naming style is **gentle birth name + inherited family/hearth surname**, with **optional earned religious or inquisitorial title/epithet**; examples are illustrative, not new NPCs. See [Races](02_Races.md), [Racial Naming Conventions and Namebook](24_Racial_Naming_Conventions_and_Namebook.md), and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). Vesperyn's religious/inquisitorial, demon-hunting and First Flame lore remains in development, and this naming approval does not set their final race stats, fire-resistance/smoke-breathing mechanics, Pyromancy ranks/costs or status in the currently defined 13-playable-race balance roster. Existing Pyromancy flame-medium canon remains unchanged.

## November 15, 2026 — Centaur full equipment attribute bonuses APPROVED

**Latest author decision:** Remove the Centaur racial penalty that halved equipment-derived numerical attribute bonuses. Centaurs now receive **100% of each otherwise legal item's normal stat bonuses**, using the ordinary stacking and rounding rules, including normal Junk accessory fractional pooling. Keep the **10% reduction to aggregate equipment Soul Strain**, rounded up once after totaling equipped sources, and all other Centaur racial properties. This explicitly supersedes the earlier 50%-bonuses rule, including the prior preserved-halving language elsewhere in this historical document. No other race, enchantment coefficient, crafted item or equipped-item bonus is changed. Comprehensive race-comparative balance testing must still include all **13 playable races**, including Centaurs.

---

## November 15, 2026 — Centaur strain affinity reduced; 13 playable races in future tests APPROVED

**Latest author approval:** Centaurs pay **90% of their combined equipped-item Soul Strain**, rounded up **once** after combining those equipment sources (ceil(0.90 × sum)). This replaces their former 50% equipment-strain discount; the former **50% reduction to equipment-derived numerical stat bonuses is now superseded; Centaurs receive full ordinary item bonuses**, as are other Centaur racial abilities. Existing ordinary Soul Strain from non-equipment sources receives no Centaur discount. **Centaurs are playable** and **must join the twelve existing playable races in future comprehensive combat/equipment balance studies**, for **13 total**. Existing older twelve-race audit results are historical samples and do not establish Centaur nonplayability. See [Centaurs and Smithcraft](07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md), [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), and [Races](02_Races.md).

---

## November 15, 2026 — Drider starting stats revised; +10% all-stat exception APPROVED

**Latest Drider canon:** Base level-1 STR/DEX/END/INT/SPD **18/18/16/15/15** (total 82), with a special **+10% permanent bonus to each of the five stats**, yielding effective **19.8/19.8/17.6/16.5/16.5** (total **90.2**). This replaces Drider's former base **19/19/19/19/19** and END +25%, and expressly overrides the general racial 25%-step modifier policy **only for Driders**. Their strong STR/DEX, moderate END, racial abilities, size, and rarity remain; no other race or combat rule is revised. See [World and Shimmer](01_World_and_Shimmer.md), [Races](02_Races.md), [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). **Previous Drider-matchup audits are snapshots of obsolete stats; future tests must recalculate.**

## November 15, 2026 — DM and ChatGPT encounter wound-route check APPROVED

**Mandatory encounter preparation:** For any fight intended to be resolved by wounding an opponent, the DM, **especially ChatGPT acting as DM**, must check that the Hero has **a realistic, legal way to inflict at least one wound** with their actual equipment/abilities, available space, targeting and enemy END/armor. No assumed weapon swaps, physically fictional exposed spots, or attacks still below **Impact >0.75** even at AR 0. If a wound route does not exist, alter the encounter or give a genuinely achievable **non-wounding win condition**. A deliberately unwoundable survival/escape threat is valid where wounding is not needed; the rule is not a guarantee of victory. See [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) and [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md). No combat numerical rules were changed.

---

## November 15, 2026 — Starter armor, Heavy armor access and DM crafting warning APPROVED

New Heroes receive their existing Junk QI Boughsteel weapon and armor grant, normally **Light armor** or **Medium armor if qualified**, but **no Heavy armor** among ordinary starting gear or in beginner shops. Heavy weapons are permitted at level 1 when otherwise lawful. Normal availability advances from readily accessible QI Medium around levels 5–9, to uncommon Partial Heavy (later in that band), more accessible Partial/Standard Heavy at levels 10–14, and Full Heavy as high-end gear at levels 15–20, with DM-controlled exceptional early access. **DM WARNING: Do not allow crafting or commissioning to bypass Heavy armor's campaign availability or actual stat/smithcraft/forge/material prerequisites.** These level bands are availability guidance, not new universal use-level gates. See [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md) and [Centaurs and Smithcraft](07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md). Targeted accessibility/combat tests remain open.

## November 14, 2026 — Soul anatomy, casting, and ordinary death

[Magic and the Soul](01_World_and_Shimmer.md#magic-and-the-soul) now covers Cognum and memory dispersal, Anima affinity and bodily spellcasting, Spiritus damage and Soul Shred, and the Corpus's role in ordinary death and possible early revival. Starspawn Celestial Recall and Warlock Soulwork retain their established exceptions. Necromancy remains a future concept.

---

## November 14, 2026 — Warlock and Moontouched Anima and Celestial Recall

Warlocks have an enlarged Anima and trace mana below 1 MP. Other Moontouched have a weak Anima that digests only enough managmins for survival. In Celestial Recall, the Astral Heart preserves the original Cognum and recreates the other three parts from recovered material and formless mana. See [Warlock Soul Anatomy](04_Warlock_Manacraft.md#warlock-soul-anatomy) and [Immortality and Celestial Recall](01_World_and_Shimmer.md#immortality-and-celestial-recall).

---

## November 14, 2026 — Four-part soul anatomy

The soul comprises **Anima** (magical manipulation, emission, and conversion of dietary managmins into pure mana), **Cognum** (the mind housed in the brain), **Spiritus** (the stabilized framework containing pure mana), and **Corpus** (the physical body anchoring Spiritus to this form). See [Magic and the Soul](01_World_and_Shimmer.md#magic-and-the-soul).

---

## November 14, 2026 — Prepared reactive defensive casting

On their own turn, casters allocate their shared Channel Limit to any offensive magic and at most **one specific prepared defensive spell** with a narrow, outcome-independent trigger. The defense uses the reserved capacity at a legitimate response opportunity, subject to medium, access and normal MP costs. **No borrowing from the next turn.** The shared budget still covers other applicable channeling. The prepared defense expires at the **beginning of the caster’s next turn**, whether used or unused. Protection beyond that point requires casting a separate spell with sufficient duration or eligible maintenance. A mixed physical and magical turn halves the applicable Channel Limit, rounds it down, and allows at most one attack total. Exact response timing and specialist interactions remain open. See [Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md), [World and Shimmer](01_World_and_Shimmer.md), and [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) for the separate physical Guard rules.

---

## November 13, 2026 — Magical media and contests (author-approved philosophy)

[Magical Media and Contests](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md) governs qualitative environmental dependencies and one-stat Power contests: medium seizure at equal effective spell Power (no additional spell on seizure turn), complete spell seizure at double Power (prior action prepared or next action surrendered). Wakecraft manipulates liquid water, Pyromancy uses flame and can convert it to smoke, and Naga Psycraft requires gem-focused Cipherlight. Existing Wakecraft Threefold Weave and Psycraft ranks need deliberate follow-up redesign; their SP/MP ledgers remain intact. Exact contest MP calculations, timing and medium quantities remain open.

## November 13, 2026 — Physical ranged benchmarks and shield constructions approved

Use Bludgeoning for Skyvein throws with the ordinary 25% covered-AR bypass on body hits. At the same legal attributes, Light single-target throw Power is **120% of comparable bow Piercing Power** and its range boundaries are **75% of that bow's**. Medium and Heavy use **100% of comparable bow Piercing Power** for one target and **50% of that bow's range boundaries**. Use Shortbow, Recurve, and Longbow as the respective ordinary bow benchmarks for the three classes. The existing 75% per target for two and 55% per target for three apply to the appropriate single-target throw Power. Existing 1/2/3 target caps, independent hit resolution, return, and full-turn loss of shield benefits apply. Exact ordinary bow range distances and detailed return-path rules remain unresolved.

The approved starting full-draw Power benchmarks for **Shortbow / Recurve / Longbow** are **702 / 1,485 / 2,681 Piercing** and **1,048 / 2,218 / 4,005 Slashing** (nearest whole values from the calibration). The corresponding draw caps used are **22 / 32 / 43**. Use the existing quadratic partial-draw rule and ordinary arrowhead bypass. These are working Power benchmarks; ordinary bow range distances remain unresolved.

Approved shield constructions are **Trufula frame with a load-bearing Boughsteel face** (physical Rating ×1.00, support STR ×0.85, with existing applicable Trufula hazards) and **Boughsteel frame with a load-bearing Glassweave face** (Balanced B, including first-physical-wound shatter). A **Nullporcelain inset on a load-bearing Boughsteel face** remains an open design question for magical interception. Boughsteel governs its physical contact. Celestite retains its Quality V triune-forging requirement. Detailed fabrication specifications and hours remain open.

See [Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md), [Ranged Catalogue](27_Ranged_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md), and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 13, 2026 — Skyvein multi-target Power split approved

The author approved **75% of the applicable single-target throw Power per target for two distinct targets**, and **55% per target for three distinct targets**. Apply the split to final Power before target defenses. The total before defenses is 150% or 165% if all contacts land. Existing Light/Medium/Heavy target caps of 1/2/3 and the full-turn forfeiture of shield benefits apply. Single-target Power and range, especially for Light, remain open. Bludgeoning and half-bow range were test assumptions. See [Physical Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md#skyvein-bow-comparison-and-approved-multi-target-split) for the landed-wound test and earlier trial.

## Shield spell interception and Skyvein bow benchmark — November 13, 2026

A usable, qualified Nullporcelain or Glassweave shield in Guard can intercept **any incoming spell effect against its bearer**, including psychic effects and the bearer's exposure to an area spell. Resolve the shield's absorption against that exposure. When the shield absorbs the entire incoming spell Power, the bearer is unaffected. When spell Power exceeds what the shield can absorb, the excess reaches the bearer and the spell resolves against them under its normal rules. An area interception protects only that bearer; the area and its other targets resolve normally. If Guard is unavailable under existing rules, including net Extreme Pressure of at least 3 PP, the spell resolves against the bearer normally. Against two genuine coordinated simultaneous flankers, the existing one-interception limit applies; the unblocked attack resolves normally.

Spell magnitude-to-Power conversion, the exact absorption and overflow calculation, partial-effect adjudication for non-damage spells, and whether magical contact causes ceramic wounds remain unresolved.

**Approved balance target:** Skyvein throws should be comparable in effectiveness to a bow with similar stat requirements, accounting for the ability to hit distinct targets and for losing all shield benefits on the throwing turn. Exact throwing Power, range bands, and return-path details remain proposals until selected. The [shield rules and draft trials](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md) own the details.

## November 13, 2026 — Shield material package and fixed Reprise Bronze Rating approved (LATEST)

See [Physical Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md) for the operative rules and [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md) for supersession.

**Reprise Bronze:** Calculate one Rating at each Quality using `round_half_up(Boughsteel Medium shield Rating × Reprise Bronze Medium armor AR / Boughsteel Medium armor AR)` with all inputs at that Quality. Quality I–V Ratings are **19/33/53/81/112**, fixed across forms. Select a prepared **Light, Medium, or Heavy** form at the start of the wielder's turn without an action. The form lasts until their next turn and governs handling, Guard obstruction, and class-based attack options, including Heavy's Double Strike restriction. Quality and installed enchantments persist. The shield and worn armor switch independently. Standard Gem cost equals Medium at **30/60/120/240/480 Gems**. All-form forging remains unresolved, and support STR ×1.00 remains a conditional engineering reference.

Balanced B applies to legal load-bearing ceramics. **Glassweave** uses **×1.25 physical Rating, ×0.95 support STR, ×1.75 magical Rating**. **Nullporcelain** uses **×1.15 physical Rating, ×0.75 support STR, ×3 magical Rating**. Apply the magical multiplier to Rating before squaring for eligible Guard contacts. First-physical-wound shatter and breaking-blow overflow still apply.

Ready **Bellcopper** multiplies final physical shield Defense by **×1.50 Medium / ×2 Heavy**. Actual condition loss after enhanced Defense charges the reservoir, disabling the multiplier until **three uninterrupted qualifying quiet turns** pass. Light shield contact is acoustically muffled. **Hearthiron** Light/Medium/Heavy bash Fire-effect budgets are **10%/20%/35%** of ordinary final bash Power. **Thornspring** holds **one Springload**, which can be spent on a melee block for **one opposing PP for one turn**, or on a bash for **+10% otherwise final bash Power**.

Open rules include magical spell eligibility and Power matching, whether magic can wound ceramic shields, Skyvein range and throwing Power, Bellcopper acoustic scope and qualifying quiet-turn criteria, Hearthiron elemental conversion and heat economy, Thornspring winding, recharge and Pressure refresh, Celestite weapon Leydrain radius and cutoff, and special-material construction, sourcing, and shield fabrication hours.

## November 13, 2026 — Shield material abilities approved in identity; historical ceramic 540-case trial (superseded above)

**Newest author decision / owning source [Physical Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md):** Nullporcelain and Glassweave shields gain eligible Guard interception of certain direct single-target spells/psychic attacks with **much stronger Nullporcelain magical Shield Rating**, physical ceramic first-wound shatter unchanged. Skyvein thrown Light/Medium/Heavy shield hits up to **1/2/3 distinct ranged targets**, returns, and supplies **no shield benefits for that entire turn**. Reprise Bronze can change shield Light/Medium/Heavy prepared form at turn start without action and replaces historical fixed ×0.90 shield Rating with the **Reprise-armor-AR/Boughsteel-armor-AR ratio at the same size/Quality**; resulting Light/Medium/Heavy Quality I–V Ratings are **14/25/44/66/90, 19/33/53/81/112, 25/45/76/116/157**. Oathsilver shield copies armor's QI–V **+3/6/9/12/15% per eligible installed enchantment** and oath-attunement principle. Bellcopper shield receives a resonant defensive reservoir; Hearthiron shield itself has **×3 Fire resistance** and size-specific fire bash concepts; Thornspring shield uses stored Springloads for melee-block Recoil Pressure for one turn and **+10% FINAL bash Power per Springload spent**, no dash. Celestite shield shares **weapon Leydrain**, but numerical weapon radius/cutoff are still absent from canon and must not be invented from armor; exceptional QV triune creation, story-warping artifact, not normal multi-item player loadout.

**Exact ceramic physical Rating/STR modifiers and magical Rating multipliers remain PROPOSALS, NOT APPROVED.** Owning shield document compares 540 controlled cases. It recommends **Glassweave physical Rating×1.25/support STR×0.95; Nullporcelain Rating×1.15/support STR×0.75** as the middle package, plus tentative **magical Shield Rating ×1.75 Glass / ×3.00 Null**; the material ability directions themselves are approved, not these new choices. Bellcopper reservoir trigger/factors, Hearthiron bash elemental magnitude, Thornspring Recoil numeric value/capacity, Skyvein range/attack profile, exotic recipes/prices, and Celestite weapon Leydrain numbers require author decision. Normal auto Guard/paired-flanker restriction, zero intrinsic shield SPD, F1.05 bash, no Heavy Double Strike and approved prices remain unchanged. See [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).
