## November 13, 2026 — Ceramic wound-shatter, Junk shield defenses, automatic Guard, half-time smith repairs APPROVED (LATEST)

**Author's latest shield rule corrections** (owner [Physical Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md), [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md)): **Ceramic load-bearing shields** should have HIGH Shield Defense for effective STR requirements, **shattering immediately upon their first true shield wound**, defined by `P≥D=(actual Shield Rating)²`; lesser contacts cause no wear. No progressive ceramic shield damage track. Use old shield-breaking residual `max(0,P−3D)` to torso once when this first contact shatters it. This overrides previous ceramic 0.75D/1.75D two-/three-loss proposal. **New ceramic numerical Rating/STR multipliers are still UNAPPROVED**; old optional Nullporcelain×0.35 and Glassweave×1.00 Rating references await rebalancing to fit the new ceramic philosophy. Genuine wearable ceramic armor's separate shatter canon is unchanged.

**Junk QI Boughsteel shield:** Two usable states **Intact→Dented→Broken**, rather than ordinary three **Intact→Dented→Cracked→Broken**; **−10% to FINAL Shield Defense Power**, `D_Junk=.90×(QI Boughsteel Shield Rating)²`, not minus 10% to Rating before squaring. Use existing shield contact thresholds on its D and cap by two durability losses; when broken, subtract the remaining Junk Shield Defense capacity once before torso injury. Existing half-QI Junk cost gives **Light7.5 Gems, Medium/Heavy15 Gems** and does not create new offhand hit/armor penalties.

**Automatic shield coverage / interception:** A legally equipped/qualified shield in **Guard automatically blocks any otherwise successful physically blockable attack**, melee or ranged, so no separate reactive timing/awareness/facing action against ordinary lone attackers. This is suspended at **Extreme Pressure net ≥3 meaningful PP** or when deliberately choosing already-approved **Deflect / Light-Medium shield Double Strike**, which trade away blocking. **Two genuine flankers with adjacent initiative positions may elect simultaneous attacks**, consuming their normal attack actions and forcing the shieldbearer to select which one to auto-intercept; other shot/strike gets ordinary hit/evasion, body END/AR and material, never an automatic hit. Existing legal voluntary initiative delay for a coordinated simultaneous exchange remains. Shield ordinary Guard obstruction+0/+1/+1 stays conditional but cannot be layered onto the same automatic Shield Defense contact as a second independent shield penalty.

**Crafting/Proficiency/time priorities:** A smith **actually capable of crafting an item** can repair it in **half its normal lawful manufacture time** to restore condition (ordinary Light/Medium/Heavy weapon base repair4/8/12h, armor16/20/28h); qualified item/workshop/material/rare forging constraints remain. **Shield fabrication hours are UNAPPROVED**, so its absolute repair hours await a baseline. Repair service pricing/supply/salvage returns to **mundane crafting and economy BACKLOG**. **Ammunition is NOT repairable except reusable throwing weapons**, without changing ordinary 120-piece forge batches. Called shots to specifically target hands, shield straps and equipment weak points go on the **future Proficiency system backlog**; ordinary exposed body and armor-gap hit precision remains unchanged. **Real complete combat hit probabilities/win rates are deferred until AFTER enchantments**; user may explore enchantments as later balancing tools but no new enchantment effect has been approved.

**Material identity warning:** Work's other shield materials had comparative Rating/STR factors, not approved specific shield variants of complete-suit Skyvein wing flight, Reprise Bronze form changes, Oathsilver attunement, Bellcopper Defense reservoirs, Hearthiron stored fire, Thornspring Springload, Nullporcelain/Glassweave shield-specific MR or Celestite Leydrain/Soul Shred. Report appendix values remain conditional test references for only legitimate constructions; do not migrate suit-only powers to a handheld shield without future approval. [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md) records exact superseded rules and remaining numerical decisions.

## November 13, 2026 — Physical shield numeric baseline complete; Work shield package AUTHOR APPROVED (LATEST)

**LATEST SHIELD NUMERIC CANON (owner [Physical Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md), combat in [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md)):** Quality I–V Boughsteel Shield Ratings **Light18/32/53/78/105, Medium24/43/71/104/140, Heavy31/55/90/135/180**. QI Boughsteel STR/DEX minima before Quality/synergy **Light10/12, Medium27/14, Heavy62/16**. Real Guard body-line obstruction **Light +0, Medium +1, Heavy +1** only when a shield actually affects an unobstructed attack line. Defense on genuine shield contact = **Rating²** independent of wearer's END/body AR; shield wear at impact attack Power below D / ≥D / ≥2D / ≥3D = **0 / 1 / 2 / 3 losses**. Intact/Dented/Cracked fully functional, breaking-blow overflow using previously approved remaining absorption once to torso. No fixed intercept arc, no free shield HP and no universal passive invulnerability.

**MATERIAL/QUALITY:** Boughsteel Rating/STR ×**1.00/1.00**; Trufula ×**0.90/0.60**; Adamantite ×**1.15/2.50**. Round defensive Rating nearest whole half-up, calculate Quality × material × sustain-synergy requirement with final ceiling and existing 75%-of-both qualification and single-largest shortfall SPD penalty. Existing shield inherent SPD remains **0%**. [Physical Shield Profiles](29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md) includes **conditional** special-material numeric references for only physically legal shield constructions and a **shield-only load-bearing ceramic shatter** mapping at 0.75D / 1.75D. These do NOT authorize exceptional material shield recipes, wearable suit-only powers or a normal Celestite non-QV item.

**TACTICS AND GEMS UNCHANGED:** Light shield costs HALF Medium, Medium=Heavy: QI–QV Light15/30/60/120/240, Medium/Heavy30/60/120/240/480 Gems. All shields can normal STR F1.05 Narrow Bludgeoning bash and Guard/Deflect; Light/Medium can qualified Double Strike with a legal weapon, Heavy cannot. No inherent shield mobility burden. Real Pressure and directional blocking still govern interception.

**OPEN NEXT PHYSICAL EQUIPMENT:** Junk shield/weakpoint treatment, shield actual repair/smithcraft labor and physically eligible exotic face/frame designs; reactive projectile interception timing/elemental-material integrity; full weapon-material and ordinary ranged completion, updated physical nonmagic encounter regression. The Work report's custom regression scripts and outputs remain in separate personal Library archives, **not committed to GitHub**. No extra magic, shot Power, hit probabilities or item availability inferred. [Changelog approval](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 13, 2026 — Light shield HALF Medium price, Heavy shields cannot Double Strike, zero inherent shield SPD APPROVED (LATEST)

**Latest explicit shield PRICE correction:** Standard finished Light shields cost exactly **50% of Medium shields**, NOT +50% over them; Medium and Heavy shield prices remain identical. Qualities I–V **Light 15/30/60/120/240 Gems**, **Medium 30/60/120/240/480 Gems**, **Heavy 30/60/120/240/480 Gems**. This supersedes the erroneously previously recorded Light 45/90/180/360/720 and all Light-shield premium references. Other established prices remain unchanged. Current operative [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md) top section corrects older historical rows.

**Latest explicit shield COMBAT approval:** Light/Medium shields may use approved weapon+shield Double Strike (two independent 60% final-Power hits, user self-PP and surrendering Guard/Deflect). **Heavy shields CANNOT Double Strike**, but may make **one normal approved STR F1.05 Bludgeoning Shield Bash** while retaining otherwise valid Guard/active blocking. All shield categories retain otherwise valid Guard and Deflect. **Light, Medium and Heavy shields impose 0% intrinsic SPD burden**; original worn armor mobility and existing shield-handling shortfall penalty remain meaningful. There is **NO proposed general two-weapon Double Strike ban** for Heavy-armored fighters simply because their worn armor is Heavy. See [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) and [Melee Weapon Catalogue](25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md).

**Still NOT approved:** Proposed Quality-I–V Light/Medium/Heavy Shield Rating ladders (including 160/225/270 at QV), physical material multipliers, class STR/DEX handling minima, test hit-category obstruction values, numeric durability-loss conversion. Existing three-state durability track and only-on-break overflow remain in force. The latest user requested a material-level outstanding-equipment-design audit before beginning enchantments; this is a planning question, not authorization to canonize those numerical proposals. [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 13, 2026 — Double Strike and shield tactical extensions AUTHOR APPROVED (LATEST)

**CANON TWO-WEAPON DOUBLE STRIKE:** On **one attack action**, physically qualified character can make **two separate weapon attack attempts**, one per hand, each at **60% of its own completed FINAL physical Power**, versus the same legal target or up to two actually reachable targets, **one target per hand without multiplying Sweep/Wide Sweep/Line**. This replaces, rather than stacks with, the ordinary offhand **Attack** (+1 meaningful pressure against enemy when credible) or **Defense** (counter credible incoming PP). Double Strike grants **one self-applied Pressure Point lasting until start of user's next turn**. Each hand's genuine weapon primary/alternate F, attack stat, material, Quality, legal enchantment and injury form matter independently. **Latest clarified design reason:** prior offhand Attack/Defense-only modes failed to reward investing in a premium enhanced offhand versus a cheap Quality I dagger; approved separate strike gives expensive offhand equipment a real optional payoff, **without forbidding or arbitrarily debuffing daggers**.

**CANON SHIELD COMBAT:** Ordinary shield bash **STR-based Bludgeoning Narrow F1.05**, using existing physical Bludgeoning 25% AR bypass, below normal one-handed mace F1.17. **Guard:** normal selected weapon attack OR one shield-bash as the normal action, with shield user still allowed credible legal active blocking and body-hit obstruction. **Deflect:** forgo deliberate active block AND intentional shield-body obstruction in return for normal credible offhand Defense counteraction to ONE incoming Pressure Point. **Shield+weapon Double Strike:** two real attacks at respective 60% final Power, **no active block, no deliberate shield obstruction or Deflect**, with the same one self-PP. Actual incidental collision remains physically possible. There is no free shield-bash damage packet on blocking. The established 3-loss shield durability and approved break overflow persist. The earlier November13 trial report and previous absolute “no extra offhand hit” apply only historically or to normal non-Double-Strike action; current combat rule governs.

**OWNING FILES:** [Combat and Shimmer Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) (full procedure and limits), [Melee Weapon Catalogue](25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md) (per-weapon approved F and legal modes), [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md) (why premium offhand now matters, unchanged finished Gem pricing), [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md) (author approval/supersession). Shield Ratings Buckler/Standard/Tower **30/50/75**, shield-body obstruction modifiers, shield-specific attribute minima and remaining missing damage-to-durability bands **remain unapproved numeric candidates**, not silently canonized by F1.05.

**NEXT RECOMMENDED EQUIPMENT MILESTONE:** Finish **Light/Medium/Heavy shield mechanical profiles**: Quality/material-dependent defensive Ratings, STR/DEX handling gates, mobility/position, durability-loss calibration and obstruction positioning, while preserving approved identical Medium/Heavy shield prices and premium Light shields. Recheck against normal dual wield and Heavy/Medium armor specialists; no assumption that a same-price Heavy shield should always dominate a Medium shield. Magical ammo/forge/Cogloom operation remains the author-directed low-priority bucket.

## November 12, 2026 — Heavy Moderate B minimums APPROVED; STR-first Slashing build test levels 1–100 complete (LATEST)

**LATEST EXPLICIT AUTHOR APPROVAL:** The author approved the previously recommended **Moderate B Heavy melee STR/DEX prerequisites** and directed them to be recorded on GitHub. The **twelve approved Quality I Boughsteel Heavy stat minima** now govern the weapon-profile test baseline and are recorded as operative in **[`25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md`](25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md)**: Greatsword STR23/DEX15; Zweihander STR25/DEX16; Greataxe STR24; Maul STR24; Great hammer STR25; Poleaxe STR22/DEX15; Halberd STR22/DEX15; Glaive STR21/DEX14; Pike STR20/DEX14; Long spear STR16 **OR** DEX16 with DEX-mode Support STR8; Lance STR24; Heavy flail STR25. Prior November6 **Heavy** lower requirements and exploratory Moderate A/High proposals are superseded for these gates. The November6 **Light/Medium** replacement minima remain working test baselines, not newly finalized by this Heavy-only decision. Legal post-material/workmanship effective-stat gates, racial multipliers and actual equipment eligibility still apply. No level-based unlock is introduced. **All 31 individually approved primary F values and current real secondary F ratings remain unchanged.**

**NEW AUTHOR-SPECIFIED DAMAGE PHILOSOPHY (DESIGN GOAL):** Author now wants, in general, **dedicated STR highest Slashing damage by a meaningful margin, STR+DEX intermediate, dedicated DEX lowest**, the latter compensated by ranged versatility and enemy-movement prediction; this replaces the prior **STR+DEX should have the highest** aspiration. The author requested full best-legal-Slashing-weapon calculations at **L1/10/20/40/60/80/100**. **No replacement governing-attack formula, F nerf/buff, ability bonus or actual hit probability has been approved** merely by expressing the desired hierarchy. The current operative `A_Both=0.65(effective STR+effective DEX)` and `Power=(A×complete approved F)^2` remain intact, with genuine forms and armor behavior unchanged.

**DETERMINISTIC 231 best-eligible single-Slashing-strike states:** Compare **11 canonical Hero races × 7 levels × 3 investment paths** (+2 STR per level; +1 STR/+1 DEX; +2 DEX; every-fifth all-stat bonus). All builds at L1 same-race **tie** before distinct allocated gains. Using QI Boughsteel, latest approved Heavy minima, November6 working Light/Medium minima and strongest physically allowed Slash, strict STR > hybrid > DEX ordering succeeds in **8/11 races at L10**, **8/11 at L20**, **3/11 at L40**, **3/11 at L60**, **3/11 at L80**, **3/11 at L100**. At L100 STR leads in **9/11** but DEX exceeds hybrid in **8/11**. Thornguard L100: STR Greataxe **Power201,063** > hybrid Zweihander **78,861** > DEX Sabre **71,182**. Neutral-STR/DEX Drider L100: STR Greataxe **129,773** > DEX Sabre **74,284** > hybrid Zweihander **64,897**. STR-disadvantaged Bloomkin and Corvyn L100 permit DEX-top racial exceptions. Drider hybrid and DEX L40 can get exactly equal legal Zweihander Power because Both `A=0.65(STR+DEX)` depends only on sum, not attribute distribution.

**Full comprehensive results:** [`26_Melee_Power_Calibration_DRAFT.md`](26_Melee_Power_Calibration_DRAFT.md) owns all seven checkpoint tables, all-race L100 outcomes, legal Slashing attack selection, material/Quality sensitivity and proposed solutions. Hypothetical QV Adamantite gear does not solve the high-level hybrid/DEX reversal (strict desired ordering still 3/11 at L100). Actual open Wide Sweep can benefit squad aggregate versus Sabre Sweep if full target geometry exists, but it cannot be applied as automatic individual Power, free action, or hit chance.

**RECOMMENDATION, NOT APPROVED:** Keep the just-approved weapon F roster and Heavy minima for now; investigate conditional STR-sensitive or balance-aware **Both-stat** Power calibration and compensate weapon Fs/armor benchmarks in a separate author-approved pass. Example **UNAPPROVED** `A_Both=0.85 STR+0.65 DEX` improves raw desired ranking from 28/66 to 51/66 across L10–100/11 races but **breaks earlier greatsword Point Impact target** (L20 reference ~1.635→~2.27 with unchanged F); do NOT implement it. Simply nerfing DEX Sabre doesn't cure equal-sum access to legal Both weapons, and enforcing a strict universal order risks erasing deliberate permanent racial advantages. No new combat formula, Power, Hit, F, AR or Guard values adopted from a test.

**GitHub authority/status:** Latest approved Heavy QI Boughsteel prerequisites, latest STR-first philosophy and the **unapproved** full damage battery all recorded to `main`; `10_Canon_Changelog.md` records exact approval and supersession. Weapon catalogue F, modes, cross-stat handling and secondary factors remain subject only to their own previously approved rules. Pending author decision: what amount of hybrid specialization in physical Slashing to reward, whether racial exceptions are desired, and whether to authorize a joint Both-formula/weapon-F/armor retuning; not a prerequisite to using the newly approved Heavy gates.

## November 12, 2026 — Greatsword/maul/pike varied battle battery; blade-tip secondary Piercing ×0.80 APPROVED (LATEST)

**LATEST EXPLICIT USER-CONDITIONED WEAPON BALANCE DECISION:** The author requested realistic varied testing (open fight, corridor, group, reach, armor, material and stat specialization) and authorized a Power-only secondary-Piercing adjustment **if needed**, with a greatsword-thrust **~1.6 Impact** target. In the established level20 Thornguard B-attacker versus E-defender, **QII Boughsteel Full Medium AR52** working class/shape comparison, greatsword primary Slashing yielded **Impact1.408**, its old **×0.82 blade-tip** thrust **1.707**, primary pike Piercing **1.798**, and maul Bludgeoning **1.639**. The greatsword secondary was too close to dedicated Piercing given its alternative broad Slashing attack. Apply the modest approved **new blade-tip secondary factor ×0.80 of its already-adjusted primary F**, producing **Impact1.624**, close to the author's ~1.6 target. It is **not proof of universal greatsword dominance** or a per-weapon-F ratification.

**NO ARMOR PENETRATION CHANGE:** Legitimate genuine Piercing primary and secondary attacks still bypass **50% of covered AR**, earned genuine rear adds **10 percentage points**; purpose-built secondary spike/beak remains **×0.90**, not globally nerfed; other primary damage forms and armor class/material/Quality/STR/SPD, Shimmer Guard, XP/Gems, Junk, accessory half-point final-floor, geometry, actions and shield rules remain intact. The older ×0.82 on November11 and in the immediately preceding November12 three-path tests is **HISTORICAL/SUPERSEDED for BLADE-TIP POWER only**. Specific catalogue F values remain DRAFT, and the exact 1.624 ratio depends on the expressly specified reference normalization, not all real greatswords.

**81 eligibility-screened conditional landed-hit comparisons in levels1–20:** all 81 raw Impact ratios shrink, **four** cross downward one injury stage. Arc-group fights favor greatsword/maul Wide Sweep (three genuine forward targets when clearance/reach exists), straight depth-aligned corridors favor the pike's Line (up to three genuinely unobstructed in-line targets); cramped clearance can prohibit Wide Sweep entirely, leaving greatsword's Point thrust as a one-target alternate. Genuine Short grapples inside Long reach shut down both ordinary Long weapon modes, and Long-versus-Short approach retains its approved **SPD1.25/1.50** thresholds subject to actual Pressure. No dice/win percentages invented. Level50 stronger-weapon material checks preserve dedicated Piercing importance, while the draft warhammer/mace purpose-built beaks/spikes do not require modification.

**Full owning report** [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md); **operative hit/Power source** [Combat and Guard](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md); **weapon draft arithmetic and coverage** [Melee Weapon Catalogue](25_Melee_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md); **approval and supersession** [Canon Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). **Still unresolved:** final individual per-weapon Fs and handling/minimums, formal hit success odds, real-world probabilities, shield and specialized tactical edge cases, and any later attack-form injury multiplier. ## November 12, 2026 — Three-path weapon/armor/stat balance follow-up TESTED (EXPLORATORY; NO RULE CHANGES)

The author approved running the next three physical equipment balancing paths: **(1) Bludgeoning/Slashing/dedicated Piercing/secondary Piercing, (2) armor Quality versus Light/Medium/Heavy class, (3) STR/SPD versus STR/END versus STR/DEX builds**. Twenty levels and eligibility-checked conditionally landed hits were examined against the latest approved rules; see full methods, tables and qualification cautions in **[`09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md)** and status in **[`10_Canon_Changelog.md`](10_Canon_Changelog.md)**. This records test authorization and findings, not new mechanical approvals.

**Salient findings:** The **current** secondary blade-tip rule is **50% Piercing AR bypass with ×0.82 of adjusted primary F** (not the older 25%/×0.90). When compared at the same class and attack attribute, generic secondary Piercing is almost equal in raw impact to generic dedicated Piercing; the actual greatsword's ×0.875 shape adjustment and the pike's ×0.90 shape adjustment give the pike only ~5.3% more Power at equal Both-stat A, with meaningful thresholds in particular armor matchups. Across 20 levels, moving **Light QI→Medium QI reduces injury in 44/72 legal reference states** versus **Light QI→QII only 8/80**; higher armor class has a real inherent SPD-evasion cost. STR/SPD improves direct-hit difficulty, STR/END mitigates landed harm, and STR/DEX increases Both-stat attack output but risks lower SPD. **Recommend keeping current approved numbers and testing item-specific attack geometry/F and the greatsword/pike niche further.** No hit percentage, actual win-rate, or new F/AR values have been authorized. Earlier Junk accessories final-floor, Junk hit-category modifiers, Hero starter grants and W100=3200 Gem model are unchanged.

## November 12, 2026 — Junk accessory half-points pool; levels 1–20 encounter tests completed (LATEST)

**Latest explicit author-approved rule:** Junk accessory numeric attribute bonuses contribute their actual **half-value fractional contributions**, including **+0.5**. **Pool contributions across the legal equipped Junk accessories for each SAME attribute, THEN floor the resulting final affected stat only once**. Two +0.5 contributions give +1; three +0.5 give +1.5, so final stat20→21 when other terms are integral. Do not truncate each item early or round fractions up. Preserve **already approved Centaur-specific item-total reduction and rounding** as a distinct exception; mixed case needs a later check. This is an accessory computation rule, not a global rewrite of unrelated fractional racial attributes or unfinished enchantment-capacity, rune/socket and future implement mechanics.

**Levels 1–20 combat requested and completed, 320 categorical encounter checks:** Two comparison sets with **the same player upgrades**: (A) foes ordinarily equipped from L1, (B) foes begin in Junk and mirror each player replacement at L6, L11 and L16. Player phases: **L1–5 Junk weapon+armor**, **L6–10 normal QI weapon and Junk armor**, **L11–15 ordinary QI both**, **L16–20 QII weapon and ordinary QI armor**. Two example Thornguard attribute allocations (STR/SPD versus STR/END) and four synthetic equal-level enemy profiles (peer, +30% SPD, +25% END/−25% SPD, +60% SPD) produce **20 × 2 × 2 × 4 = 320** combat states. No fixed hit percentages/dice exist in canon: these are category and conditional landed-impact tests, NOT claimed statistical win probabilities or actual campaign combats.

**Headline result (peer difficulty: Hero outgoing / enemy incoming):** L1–5 normal gear foe **Hard/Easy**, mirrored Junk foe **Average/Average**; L6–10 normal foe **Average/Easy**, mirrored upgraded foe **Easy/Easy**; from L11 onward both tracks **Average/Average** in otherwise symmetric equipment. A fast (+30% SPD) normally equipped foe during early levels can drive attacks against Junk defender to **Certain**, while the Junk Hero must land **Very Hard** attacks at L1; a very fast (+60% SPD) foe can create **Impossible/Certain** at L1 until legitimate tactical openings or retreat. Important: a level1 END-heavy bruiser may simply resist an early melee attack as Superficial even if the Hero connects.

**Separate level16 warning, not a Junk rule defect:** A legal STR/SPD-invested character with a QI Full Light Boughsteel armor opponent and working medium blunt hit reaches initial landed-hit Impact Ratio **>3** by L16, resulting in **three injury stages** on successful contact; such light armor was intentionally not upgraded in this test. A STR/END-invested opponent instead has a much lower ≈1.49 ratio at L16, remaining Solid. This illustrates the need for later armor Quality/attribute investment comparisons, not immediate Power/AR rebalancing. Hero's five-stage Guard versus ordinary foe's three stages remains unchanged.

**References:** Owning full methodology, tables, legal limitations and conditional all-contact stress cases: `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`. Combat ladder: `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md`. Approval status: `10_Canon_Changelog.md`. The Junk weapon +1 harder and armor −1 easier shifts, free Junk starter pair (even above W1=30), 50%-QI Junk pricing, and 50% reduced accessory enchantment potential all remain approved. **Still unresolved:** numerical actual hit percentages (not currently designed), accessory enchant/socket odd-fraction handling, Centaur multi-item round-order edge cases, Junk shield handling, future implements, exact item purchase prices, and playtest adjustments if any.

## November 12, 2026 — Junk equipment drawbacks and starting Junk weapon/armor APPROVED (LATEST)

**Latest author-approved equipment rule:** Junk is **Quality I, Boughsteel-based, half the equivalent standard QI Gem value**. A Junk weapon imposes **+1 harder hit/evasion-difficulty category** for attacks made using it. Junk worn armor ensemble imposes **−1 easier hit/evasion-difficulty category on incoming ordinary evadable attacks**. Each source applies only once per attack; Junk weapon versus Junk armor offsets. Do not reduce QI material weapon Power, armor AR/MR, add bonus physical requirements, a speculative damage/breakage meter, or hit-success percentage dice. The rule integrates with the six existing **Certain / Easy / Average / Hard / Very Hard / Impossible** categories and their ordinary cover/range/Aim/Pressure checks. Multiple Junk armor subpieces do **not** compound the evasion penalty. Junk shield intercept/parry details are not yet resolved.

**Junk accessories and future Junk magic implements:** **50% of normal Quality I numeric bonuses and enchantment potential**, with rounding/odd effects/partial sockets still unresolved, not a new effect or automatic discounted Soul Strain. Implements remain for later design; only this price/capacity philosophy is now approved. Physical form, Quality I and material mechanics remain unchanged except the explicitly approved handling/bonus rules. Nonmetal accessory construction details remain open.

**Starting Heroes:** Grant **one Junk weapon and one Junk protective outfit** free as starting *equipment*, chosen to meet character anatomy and usability. **The two starter items may appraise ABOVE the current W1=30-Gem normal portfolio planning benchmark**, and the author explicitly allows this intentional exception. Do not subtract their value from an ungranted wallet, add their appraised value as liquid Gems, force an initial resale, or grant automatic Gem Shop 50% buyback (only provably shop-sold gear qualifies). Later W10=200 and W25=400 economic reference targets unchanged.

**Nonexhaustive preliminary checks:** Using illustrative ordinary QI Primary Item **60 Gems**, armor **40**, Junk equivalents **30/20** and other Junk support slots **75**, a given starter pair values **50 Gems**; level10 full Junk pair/support **125**, QI-weapon-first **155**, QI-armor-first **145** and QII-primary-first with full Junk support **215 > W10=200**, requiring 15 of support sacrifice. At level25, a QIII primary or substantially enchanted QII primary plus Junk armor and accessories values **335 ≤ W25=400**. Same Average base evasion shifts Hard for Junk weapon, Easy for Junk armor, and back to Average when both apply. **All component numbers here are exploratory category allocations, not guaranteed vendor prices or complete legal enchantment packages.**

**Canon source:** `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` has equipment ownership and level-based tests; `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md` has the approved six-step hit interaction; `10_Canon_Changelog.md` records approvals. Previously unresolved mechanical Junk disadvantages are now **RESOLVED only as described above**; special shield uses, accessory rounding, magic implements and actual price/model validation remain open. Material-neutral Gem pricing, Celestite constraints, 30% Primary Item budget and ~×2 substantial enchantment premium continue unchanged.

## November 12, 2026 — Material-neutral Gem prices canonized; Celestite premium removed; purity strictly narrative (LATEST)

**LATEST AUTHOR EXPLICIT APPROVAL SUPERSEDES PREVIOUS MATERIAL-COST GUIDANCE:** For **matching equipment functional design/weapon form, armor class and coverage, workmanship Quality and enchantments**, changing **material does not change the standard Hero Gem-equivalent value**. Adamantite and all other legal material choices do not receive a material Power/rarity/weight/crafting-alloy markup or cheaper discount in the Gem economy. Their meaningful differences remain all established **stat prerequisites, mass/mobility, AR/MR, weapon F, hazards, specialties, attunement, availability and qualification**. Different true mechanisms/size or independently installed enchantments may have different form/feature prices, not a backdoor material surcharge. This is NOT proof all materials are equally available through mundane merchants or a universal forge recipe.

**CELESTITE 1.20× PREMIUM NOW RETIRED:** Same-class, same-coverage, same-Quality V Celestite armor's **nominal Gem appraisal is ×1.00 identical to matching Adamantite**, NOT the earlier +20% and not historical ×5. **Celestite remains QV only; ordinary Gem purchase is NOT AVAILABLE.** Real Celestite, extraordinarily skilled Centaur smith and **the simultaneous personal cooperation of Arrest, Surrender and Baphomet** are mandatory. Its intrinsic 5/10/15 wearer MP-per-turn drain by armor class, Leydrain fields, soul erosion per voluntary combat turn, and all other approved restrictions remain. No new buyback permission, divinity-for-Gems exchange or alternative path to Celestite production.

**Material purity: only fiction, never a game mechanic.** The author expressly clarifies that refining/purity/impurity explanations are lore illustrating how a Quality I Adamantite sword could use a lower refined alloy; purity does **not** create another rating, percentage-based Power, STR/DEX threshold, Quality rank, material restriction, or actual new combat mechanic. Separately, Heroes may acquire mundane currency, and **Centaur smiths can create and enchant gear without spending any divine Gems**. A future parallel mortal economy will consider true raw metal quantity/purity, smith labor and rarity, including conceptual one-pure-bar-to-many-alloyed-swords versus multiple-bars-for-massive-greatswords, with **no approved numeric recipe, alloy ratio, bar price or Gem exchange rate** yet.

**Focused comparisons in owning `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`:** QV Heavy reference Boughsteel vs Adamantite at STR40 Power ~3,639 vs ~5,685 for a material F ×1.25 (**56.25%** gain once both meet their minimum STR); Full Heavy armor at QV Boughsteel **AR115/STR36/−20% SPD** versus Adamantite **AR170/STR91/−30% SPD**, Nullporcelain anti-magic niche, breakable Glassweave, temporary-defense Bellcopper and dangerous rare Celestite. The economic rule is sound as a purchasing principle, but **separate material mechanics balance is not proven**. Prior material-dependent 12-pure/6-composite Gem price trials and Celestite 1.20 Gem markup are **SUPERSEDED AS PRICES**. The previous author-approved 11-slot equipment framework, W100=3,200 Gem budget, Junk QI Boughsteel gear, one-tier-up 60% shopping philosophy and enchanted Primary Item ~2× remain intact.

**References:** [Equipment & Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Centaurs & Smithcraft](07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). Older text explicitly documenting Celestite +20% and material-specific price ladders is a preserved superseded historical trail, **not operative merchant/crafting Gem tariff**.

## November 12, 2026 — Primary Item enchants and Junk Quality I equipment (LATEST APPROVAL)

**Approved working early-to-endgame shopping rules:** Primary Item is the Hero's highest-priority **one item**, usually weapon but may be armor/shield. Ordinary expected Quality I–V primary spending **60/120/240/480/960 Gems**, ≈30% of tier wealth **200/400/800/1600/3200**. A normal one-tier-ahead primary item costs ~60% of current-tier wealth; two tiers ahead costs ~120% (the author explicitly retracted their earlier suggestion that two tiers ahead could cost 60%). **A substantial enchanted package about doubles the same item/Quality's normal price**: typical enchanted primary **120/240/480/960/1920 Gems**. This does not eliminate Soul Strain or enchantment constraints, and is not Quality VI. The prior 3040-Gem eleven-slot sample counted some magical costs already, so it must be redesigned when using 1920-Gem enhanced main item rather than double-charging.

**Junk equipment category APPROVED:** Junk is always **Quality I, Boughsteel-based**, priced **50% of comparable ordinary Quality I item**, exemplified by a 30-Gem Junk primary versus 60-Gem standard QI primary. It is an inferior equipment condition/category, not a sixth Quality; exact poor durability/repair/handling/combat effects and use on nonmetal accessories remain open for separate approval. Early Heroes can fill support slots cheaply or strategically leave them open while specializing.

**OPEN AUTHOR QUESTION, NO NEW MATERIAL-PRICE APPROVAL:** Material-independent normal Gem tariff for same actual form/coverage/Quality (Adamantite vs Boughsteel) has been proposed, potentially including eliminating Celestite's previously approved 20% crafting-valuation premium. The material pricing decision is not yet complete: **retain Celestite crafting valuation 1.20× comparable Adamantite** and its strictly QV-only triune divine forging/no conventional vendor sale until explicitly superseded. Material multipliers, requirements, physical/magic defenses, hazards, rarity and divine forging aren't changed by the newly approved Junk/enchanted primary shopping principles. Read `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` and `10_Canon_Changelog.md` for status.

## November 12, 2026 — New Gem denomination and W100=3,200 equipment economy (LATEST USER DIRECTION)

**Latest explicit author instruction:** Take the previous **320-Gem level-100 reverse-engineering test**, multiply **EVERY Gem price/value in that new model by ×10**, adopt it as the new equipment-pricing foundation, introduce a small denomination for awards below one whole Gem, and reprice existing categories in accordance with the 30%-primary-item/one-tier-up≈double-spend philosophy. This **SUPERSEDES the historical W100=4,000 economy** (NOT W100=40,000); the previous 400-Gem QV maul and 480–500-Gem Greatbow ideas are also obsolete price exercises.

**Current AUTHOR-DIRECTED WORKING portfolio:** Starting **level1 30 Gems**; Quality I at level10 **200 Gems**, II25 **400**, III50 **800**, IV75 **1,600**, V100 **3,200**. The target **Primary Item** investment is **60/120/240/480/960 Gems** at these quality milestones (30% of equipment wealth). It may be weapon, armor or shield, but no price increases simply from the player's labeling. A representative item one tier up consumes ~60% of the lower-tier equipment budget. `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` owns the interpolated level-by-level reference wealth and reference full replacement/lifetime Gem calculation.

**Whole-Gem and subunit currency:** **100 Glints = 1 Gem**, providing integer Glint awards for small patron-qualified rewards while keeping Gems whole. There is no conversion to mundane coin or automatic Gem reward for ordinary quests. Ordinary 50% Gem-Shop-item-only buyback and 30%-of-lifetime-gross-earnings consumables goal remain. Reference level100 **cumulative resale loss 1,500 Gems**, **lifetime earned ~6,714.29 Gems**, **consumables ~2,014.29 Gems**. Without eligible resale for replaced portfolios, a comparable 3,200-Gem final portfolio and same 30%-consumables share could require **~8,857.14 lifetime Gems**, a scenario rather than a payout entitlement.

**11-slot complete example, GEM numbers now ×10:** six QV plus five QIII item purchases: primary **960**, armor **640**, offhand/shield/backup **320**, head **60**, neck **240**, arms **60**, hands **60**, feet **70**, waist **240**, ring A **320**, ring B **70** = **3,040 Gems spent**, **160 Gems under W100**. Illustrative installed Soul Strain **50** for natural MP100 remains an *unapproved enchantment allocation*, not an automatic purchased buff. Existing physical Quality, AR, combat F/Power, MP, SP and race-specific strain rules unchanged. Protect the old 11-slot armor ensemble's integrated coverage and legitimate two-handed handling.

**Named pricing source updates:** The Moonlit Garden's previously approved 1/2/3-Gem starter items now read **10/20/30 current Gems**; Willow Shortbow with 12 arrows is **20 Gems**, starter Hand Crossbow 12 bolts **30 Gems**. A **40 ordinary arrows** Gem Shop refill is **10 current Gems**, standard quiver still 20 arrows. Existing Shimmer paid inventory, cartography and other active conveniences converted ×10. Cogsong Command Loom former **12/28/55/90/140** becomes **120/280/550/900/1400 current Gems** by rank/quality as a pure denomination conversion, with 60/120/240/480/960 an *additional NONCANON full-game price candidate* if the Loom becomes a main build's Primary Item. Historical independent Moonlit Forge +1–+5 price system remains retired.

**NEW MATERIAL/WEAPON PRICE TRIALS ARE NOT AUTHOR-APPROVED ITEM RATES:** The owning Equipment document now contains proposed category costs across Quality I–V, pure-material & 6 composite QV full-armor comparison prices (including user-approved **Celestite QV =1.20× equivalent Adamantite crafting valuation**), and a possible nonmagical QV ordinary Boughsteel Heavy melee core **640 Gems**, QV high-draw/Titan/Colossal/Greatbow physical **320/480/640/800 Gems**, respectively. This Greatbow would be 25% above the 640-Gem ordinary Heavy core. They are **working calculation proposals** only, **not current vendor prices**, and depend on actual forge/enchant labor, accessory quality effects, rarity and user's next approvals. Crucial construction-price safeguard: STR400 high-cap Greatbow's cap persists at low workmanship Quality, so the generic 2×/tier spending ladder cannot simply make a QI Greatbow cost QV/16; its complex engine needs its own nonzero construction floor independent of Quality. No new bow weapon mechanics, attack stats or ammo compatibility were approved by this economic model.

**Precedence:** The original 3 + 3997 × ((L−1)/99)^2.3 power-curve wealth reference, all old QV 4,000-Gem portfolio notes, former 14/30/50/70/90-Gem universal price benchmark and older bow quotes remain **historical and superseded for the present Gem economy**. Retain actual approved Quality physical effects and original Soul Strain/bonus numbers. Do NOT blindly multiply high-priced legendary/quality fees ×10; they require functional rebalancing under the new wealth budget. References: [Equipment & economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [World & Shimmer](01_World_and_Shimmer.md), [Centaurs & Smithcraft](07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md), [Ranged catalogue](27_Ranged_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Canon changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). Historical campaign snapshots remain original-denomination records; future import must explicitly convert old wallet amounts rather than rewriting past roleplay/transactions.

**Open balancing caution:** The inherited stepwise expected-resale-loss formula creates a **~1,234.29-Gem earnings-budget jump at level 99→100**; the owning economy document recommends (UNAPPROVED) accruing reserves during each earlier tier while keeping milestone totals identical, which would make level99→100 ≈137.14 Gems. A 30-Gem starter portfolio sold at 50% adds another potential 15-Gem reference replacement loss if actually sold, and is omitted from the simple user-selected tier table. `campaigns/slate/README.md` carries a historical wallet conversion note; no campaign save transactions were rewritten. External historical `16_Moonlit_Garden_Gem_Shop.md` does not exist on current main, so its older unseen catalogue cannot be automatically converted.

## November 12, 2026 — Level-100 complete-loadout affordability target; Celestite crafting valuation clarified (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**Economy design benchmark:** A level-100 Hero should be economically able to assemble **armor, a primary weapon, a viable offhand weapon or shield and approximately one piece per approved equipped item slot**, with **about half top-tier and half mid-tier pieces**, capable of spending approximately **half of available natural MP capacity in equipment Soul Strain** for an eligible mana-bearing character. Actual slot chart, source-dependent strain limitations, physical quality versus enchantment packages and appropriate race/build exceptions must be respected. This is an **author-directed test objective, not a new slot list or fixed item price table**. Existing current equipment portfolio target **W100=4,000 Gems** and 30%-of-lifetime-earnings consumables budget remain approved; the author suspects affordability desynchronization. **Audit finds calibration missing rather than proving the W100 curve wrong:** e.g. provisional **1,750 Gem QV Greatbow uses 43.75%** of W100; Adamantite armor/shield/other completed item prices, full equipment slots and final enchantment costs are not approved. Old I–V base 14/30/50/70/90 Gem values do not equal completed bespoke gear prices. Prioritize multi-slot complete-loadout budgets before selecting Adamantite armor prices. Continue to pause further ammunition economics.

**Celestite author clarification:** The previously approved **5× same-class, same-coverage Quality V Adamantite armor valuation** is **for extraordinary crafting value/pricing purposes**, not ordinary shop inventory and not proof that Celestite cannot be forged. A duly qualified Smith **can forge Celestite with essential divine aid**, preserving the established triune cooperation of Arrest, Surrender and Baphomet, Centaur forging demands, materials and special risks. Celestite is still not ordinarily purchasable. Adamantite base prices are not yet defined, so no numeric Celestite crafting valuation can be computed. See [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Centaurs and Smithcraft](07_Centaurs_and_Smithcraft.md), [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

**Working bow profiles approved:** advanced high-draw / Titan / Colossal composite / legendary greatbow test slots use engineered draw caps **100/200/300/400**, legal minimum controlled draw **STR35/80/125/180**, and Handling DEX **30/35/40/45** respectively. This is the revised moderate-DEX calibration for legendary STR specialists, superseding the prior too-steep DEX proposal. Physically compatible arrows have safe launch-force ratings (working **100/200/300/400** tiers); they can be intentionally fired at **reduced actual draw** from stronger bows, but never above the arrow rating or below the bow's controlled draw minimum. Apply approved quadratic Power at actual draw; one arrow per ordinary bowshot and normal nock/draw pace, no universal Quality Power multiplier, and no new extra reload tax.

**Scope guard:** Existing high-draw shot Power **14,500 approved**. New legendary bow **45,000 / 90,000 / 150,000 shot Powers remain trial proposals**; material recipe, price, arrow geometry, ranges and final names remain open. See [Ranged Catalogue](27_Ranged_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md), [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md). Deferred ultra-late-game armor/END rebalancing is still off the current docket.

Preserve **each bow's own engineered draw cap and calibrated full-draw Power**, approved quadratic partial-draw formula and DEX-governed aim; the existing advanced high-draw bow's **proposed cap100** and approved **14,500 Power** are not silently replaced. ## November 11, 2026 — Advanced optics have target-only magical glint and approved stability/attachment handling (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

Advanced precision sights retain effective **DEX100, true bracing/stability and a spent Aim action** for one additional category of Far/Extreme range-penalty relief. **Minor stable adjustments** can preserve the bonus; **movement breaking the brace** removes the optic bonus but can preserve ordinary Aim if target tracking survives; **major tracking/shot disruption** loses both. Sights need compatible fitting and outside-combat initial calibration, have meaningful but not yet priced specialist maintenance, and can lose enhanced function from genuinely damaging impacts or misalignment; no new blanket STR/recoil/setup penalty or automatic breakage. **Distinct magical glint is perceivable only by the intended target** and can reveal the sniper's position to that target. A survivor of the opening shot who can communicate can **call out the sniper's position**, giving allies natural tactical opportunities; allies cannot see the glint themselves. No free dodge/attack/PP, magical teamwide revelation or bypass of ordinary visibility and attack rules. See [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md), [Ranged Catalogue](27_Ranged_Weapon_Catalogue_DRAFT.md), [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md), and [Changelog](10_Canon_Changelog.md).

## November 11, 2026 — Unified Piercing bypass and secondary thrust ratings (author approved)

All genuine Piercing attacks use **50% base AR bypass**, including eligible secondary thrusts. A secondary blade-tip thrust uses **×0.82 of the weapon's adjusted primary F**. A purpose-built secondary spike or beak uses **×0.90 of primary F**. Apply the factor once, with the attack's applicable quality and material factors. The actual blade tip, spike, or beak must support the attack. An earned rear hit can raise Piercing bypass to 60% under the existing rear rule.

The greatsword's Point thrust uses ×0.82 of its adjusted Slashing primary F, equivalent to relative form F0.902 before its shared ×0.875 shape factor. This supersedes the former ×0.90 / 25% thrust profile and the earlier calibration goal that the thrust always trail a comparable maul against plate. The maul's primary remains Bludgeoning Wide Sweep at 25% base bypass. Absolute class and material factors remain provisional.

See [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md) and [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md).

## November 11, 2026 — Earned rear-hit armor bypass increased (author approved)

A qualifying rear hit adds **10 percentage points** to applicable armor bypass, capped at 100%. Apply the resulting bypass to covered AR before squaring its contribution to Defense Power. A base 50% attack therefore reaches 60%. Surprise or a tactically earned rear opening is still required, including appropriate meaningful Pressure when it actually exposes the rear. Merely standing behind an alert opponent does not qualify. This supersedes the former 5-point bonus. The separate specialized Piercing profile remains retired.

See [Combat](22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md) and [Equipment](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md).

## November 11, 2026 — Line coverage and pike Piercing revision (author approved)

**Line coverage.** One Line attack may damage up to three distinct, physically reachable targets positioned in a straight line outward from the wielder. Line should be harder to execute and somewhat weaker than Sweep. Sweep retains its two-target cap in the forward arc. Use Sweep's individual-target resolution. Each eligible hit uses normal attack Power against that target's own Defense. See Combat for physical obstruction. The squad rule above now governs Line. Penetration Proficiencies remain unresolved.

**Pike and primary Piercing.** Retire the separate specialized Piercing profile (F0.85 and 65% AR bypass). The Heavy, Long, Both-stat pike uses ordinary primary Piercing (relative F0.90 and 50% AR bypass) with a pike-specific ×0.90 shape factor applied once. Its combined relative F is 0.81 before class, material and quality. When qualified, use A = 0.65 × (effective STR + effective DEX). Reference test minima are STR14 and DEX11. Final requirements and absolute class, material and individual weapon Fs remain under calibration.

## Celestite Soul Shred requires voluntary fighting; Leydrain 5/10/15 stacks — November 10, 2026 (LATEST)

**AUTHOR APPROVES:** Light/Medium/Heavy Celestite Leydrain **radius 5/10/15 meters, strict spell suppression threshold under 5/10/15 MP**, respectively. MP wearer drain/turn remains 5/10/15. Overlapping Leydrain effects STACK; working implementation adds thresholds in overlapping regions rather than radii (the exact weapon values remain unresolved). **Soul Shred** is the official name for the fixed-snapshot **5% XP loss per active combat turn**, triggered only once the wearer chooses to fight back/participate, not by wearing/traveling or being attacked while refusing to engage. **0 MP means 0 MP**, with no extra punishment. See Changelog/Smithcraft/Equipment/Combat; final edge trigger details remain open.



## November 10, 2026 — Celestite Leydrain tiers, additive overlaps, voluntary-combat Soul Shred and no zero-MP extra punishment (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**LATEST SPECIFIC APPROVAL OVERRIDES EARLIER CELESTITE LEYDRAIN <5 AT ALL ARMOR CLASSES:** Celestite armor's innate Leydrain **radius and strict spell-channel cancellation threshold** scale by protection class: **Light: radius 5m, nullifies channeled spells costing strictly LESS THAN 5 MP; Medium: 10m, suppresses spells costing strictly LESS THAN 10 MP; Heavy: 15m, suppresses spells costing strictly LESS THAN 15 MP.** A spell whose relevant MP cost equals the threshold is NOT canceled by that field's under-threshold rule alone. Friendly/beneficial/self-originated spells remain subject to cancellation. The existing separate automatic **wearer MP drain of 5/10/15 MP per turn** for Light/Medium/Heavy is UNCHANGED. This change is to the spell-canceling numeric threshold, not the radius or armor MP debit.

**AUTHOR APPROVES LEYDRAIN STACKING:** Multiple simultaneously active Celestite Leydrain effects, including armor+weapon or other valid Celestite items, do **not** merely pick the strongest; they combine to make heavily equipped Celestite users especially difficult to affect magically. **Provisional operational interpretation for straightforward field interaction (pending explicit author confirmation if necessary):** At a location inside multiple field radii, **add all those fields' applicable spell-suppression MP thresholds**, giving an effective strictly-below combined cutoff. E.g. within the intersection of two Heavy-armor-equivalent 15-MP fields, channeling of a spell costing LESS THAN 30 MP vanishes; outside the intersection but in only one 15-MP field, the single 15 cutoff applies. Radii represent distinct areas and do NOT automatically add their linear distances. The weapon's own numerical Leydrain threshold/radius, if not yet specified by its subject canon, remain OPEN and cannot be fabricated. This additive-threshold scheme is an implementation proposal consistent with the author's explicit stacking directive, rather than an invented previously approved weapon value.

**AUTHOR APPROVES 'SOUL SHRED' NAME AND VOLUNTARY COMBAT CONDITION:** The armor's fixed-reference accumulated-XP erosion is officially named **Soul Shred**. Celestite armor does NOT shred XP merely while worn, during travel, in passive proximity to monsters, or solely because hostile creatures initiate combat/attack the wearer. It activates in **actual combat WHEN THE WEARER CHOOSES TO FIGHT BACK / WILLINGLY ENGAGE in the conflict**, taking deliberate combat participation rather than passively being attacked as the trigger. Thereafter while actively committed to the combat engagement, **each qualifying turn** destroys **5% of the wearer's TOTAL XP snapshot taken when they first donned the armor**, with the original fixed baseline, possibly causing level regression and associated loss. Do not charge one Soul Shred turn for every ambient enemy projectile or automatically from initiative. An ineffective kobold harassing a nonretaliating Celestite traveler does not activate Soul Shred. Do not interpret the rule as allowing attack one turn then negate the cost every other turn simply by holding a defensive posture within the same continuing voluntary fight; detailed engagement-end/disengagement boundaries can be settled in combat rules. Nonviolent travel, clear nonengagement and actual withdrawal do not by themselves trigger Soul Shred. The weapon's distinct already approved XP/soul loss per stroke still stacks when wielded/used; no free exemption for actively wielding both.

**NO EXTRA FATE AT ZERO MANA (APPROVED):** Celestite armor MP drain reduces currently available MP no lower than **0 MP**. If MP reaches zero or is insufficient to cover the full listed debit, the wearer simply has zero MP, with no separate soul injury, instant incapacitation, wound, XP penalty, spell explosion or forced end of armor functionality merely because the MP debit could not be fully paid. Soul Shred and anti-magic field retain their otherwise legitimate triggers/effects; normal ordinary consequences of being unable to pay MP for one's own spells remain. The armor's special exemption from stat-shortfall due to its own induced level-down remains intact.

**UNRESOLVED EDGE CASES:** Detailed exact combat commitment trigger for purely defensive fighting, duration of an engagement or disengagement, once-per-turn scheduling and rounding, persistent original XP snapshot per wearer through removal/re-donning, how canceled spells are treated when channeled outside and entering fields, specific Celestite weapon Leydrain numeric parameters and total item stacking, and precise QV AR/MR/STR/DEX/SPD profiles (prior Quill suggested but not approved Heavy AR180/MR185/STR65). Do not invent Celestite weapon item data or distort existing godslayer per-stroke soul erosion. No XP drain should occur just from an imposed encounter with no voluntary retaliation/engagement.
## Thornspring turn-long burst and Celestite Quality V divine armor — November 10, 2026 (LATEST)

**APPROVED THORNSPRING REVISION:** Expend one permanently stored Springload charge for **the entire current turn** of class-specific SPD boost (Light +50%, Medium +75%, Heavy +35% in approved working QV profiles), usable in both offensive SPD/positioning and evasive comparisons. No additional action or weapon Power multiplier. **APPROVED CELESTITE ARMOR:** Quality V ONLY, Light/Medium/Heavy; AR slightly > matched Adamantite, MR slightly > matched Nullporcelain, STR between Boughsteel and Adamantite. MP drain/turn **5/10/15**; Leydrain radius **5/10/15 m** cancels channeling of spells costing **<5 MP**, including friendly/self/positive. First donning fixes XP erosion baseline; **5% of that starting total XP lost EACH combat turn**, can delevel; stat shortfall due to erosion does not penalize ongoing use after donning. Weapon+armor magic drain, XP drain and Leydrain **stack**; specific unprovided weapon values remain open. Listed price **5× equivalent Adamantite***, '*cannot be purchased, triune divine + extraordinary Centaur forging required'. No Quality I–IV Celestite armor. See subject files/changelog for edge cases.



## November 10, 2026 — Thornspring turn-long offensive/evasive Springload; Celestite Quality V armor approved (LATEST AUTHOR DECISION)

### Thornspring burst timing correction (APPROVED)

**SUPERSEDES one-maneuver-only duration:** When a Thornspring wearer deliberately **expends one Springload charge**, the class-specific **situational SPD bonus lasts for the ENTIRE CURRENT TURN**, not one movement, dash or dodge. This applies to applicable **offensive and evasive SPD-based combat comparisons**, including sudden charges/closing on an opponent and running away from incoming attacks where normal rules permit. Quality V working class bonuses are **Light +50%, Medium +75%, Heavy +35%** to effective situational SPD for that turn, not passive permanent SPD or an extra action. It is not itself a generic multiplicative bonus to weapon Power, an additional attack, a free hit, or an override of Trapped/otherwise illegal dodges. Existing wound, injury, equipment-shortfall, cover, pressure, reaction, action/initiative rules remain unless a separately approved rule says otherwise. The mechanism's exact applicability to attacks beyond positional/relative-speed comparison and turn-end timing remain integration details, not permission to change universal attack Power. Springload capacity one, permanent charge storage (including charges gained in combat), full-turn manual winding by wearer/ally, and genuine-hit recharge remain approved.

### Celestite divine armor (APPROVED)

**EXCEPTIONAL EXISTENCE AND AVAILABILITY:** Celestite can be forged as **Light, Medium and Heavy armor**, but **ONLY at Smithcraft/workmanship Quality V**, with NO Quality I, II, III or IV item versions or corresponding material stats. As with Celestite weapons, actual production still requires the original exceptional **triune divine forging with Arrest, Surrender and Baphomet** and extraordinary Centaur craftsmanship, not merely an ordinary Quality V smith or market purchase. This is an approved extraordinary exception to normal material tiers. Preserve the existing Celestite anti-ley-line, soul-destroying God War lore and unconditional Drakyn treasure-detection consequences.

**PHYSICAL/MAGICAL PROTECTION AND REQUIREMENTS:** At matching protective armor class, coverage and Quality V, Celestite's baseline **Armor Resistance (AR) is slightly HIGHER than Adamantite**, and its baseline **Magic Resistance (MR) slightly HIGHER than Nullporcelain**, not astronomically beyond either. Its required **STR lies between Boughsteel's and Adamantite's corresponding Quality V armor support STR**. Actual Quality V Full Heavy comparison anchors: Adamantite AR170 (working approved), Nullporcelain MR170 (working approved), Boughsteel STR36 and Adamantite STR91 (working test/support benchmarks). Exact Celestite AR/MR/STR values and appropriate other-class comparative Adamantite AR/Boughsteel/Adamantite STR and mobilities are OPEN, not numerical canon. No automatic celestial extra armor penetration, regeneration, immunity or universal MR-to-AR equation is invented.

**PASSIVE MP DRAIN, EVERY TURN WHILE WORN:** Celestite Light armor drains the wearer **5 MP per turn**; Medium **10 MP per turn**; Heavy **15 MP per turn**. This is intrinsic anti-magic/soul effect, not optional equipment enchantment expenditure. Do not mislabel it as current equipment Soul Strain. Timing of debit and behavior when the wearer has fewer MP than required remain open.

**LEYDRAIN (APPROVED):** Celestite's passive anti-spell field has radius **5 meters Light**, **10 meters Medium**, **15 meters Heavy** from the wearer. **Any spell being channeled using LESS THAN 5 MP within that field vanishes**, including wearer-authored, friendly, healing and other positive spells, not merely hostile spells. A spell channeled with exactly 5 MP does not meet this specific below-five threshold (but is not thereby automatically immune to other Celestite defenses or effects); no new blanket cancellation of high-MP spells from this numeric rule alone. Distinguish channeled spells from unrelated nonspell environmental effects until canon defines their interactions. Whether the mana count refers to single activation cost versus total channel expenditure, overlap resolution when multiple Celestite items are present, and spell created outside the radius entering the field are implementation questions, not authorization to change the approved threshold/radii.

**SOUL/EXPERIENCE DESTRUCTION WHILE IN BATTLE (APPROVED):** On the FIRST DONNING of a Celestite armor ensemble, snapshot **the wearer's TOTAL accumulated XP** as the armor's fixed erosion reference amount (do not recompute 5% from declining current XP each turn). During battle, **each turn the wearer loses 5% OF THAT RECORDED TOTAL XP**, potentially losing levels and associated normal level-derived stats/skills, matching existing Celestite direct soul-erosion lore rather than refundable Soul Strain. XP loss is a fixed baseline percentage per turn, not 5% of *remaining* XP. The author's rule specifically states **in battle**; behavior on repeated doffing/redonning, survival at XP zero, rounding fractional XP, end-of-turn ordering, and soul destruction's terminal state need careful integration. No XP recovery or moral-protection exemption is granted by armor. Wearing Celestite after leveling down **does not incur any equipment-stat-shortfall penalty caused solely by reduced STR/DEX from this Celestite XP erosion**; after it is properly put on, the armor remains operable even if the bearer can no longer satisfy the original stat requirements. This exception does not grant general stat bonuses, eligibility to newly don armor without qualifications, immunity to wounds, or exemption from unrelated equipment shortfalls. Treat other new equipment or unrelated requirements under existing rules.

**STACKING WITH CELESTITE WEAPON (APPROVED):** If the wearer simultaneously wields a Celestite weapon, the weapon's existing **MP drain (if any applicable from the weapon's own canon), XP/soul drain per stroke, and anti-ley-line/Leydrain capability** apply **IN ADDITION** to armor's passive MP drain, battle-per-turn XP erosion and Leydrain. They do not cancel or merge for a discount. This is an additive danger and stacking design directive; exact numerical weapon MP drain and field threshold/radius stack resolution have **NOT** been separately provided in the existing weapon subject canon and must NOT be invented. Existing approved weapon soul-erosion per stroke and False Apotheosis/conviction peril are retained. Any two anti-magic field overlap effects should be respected rather than silently selecting only the better source; exact additive distance/threshold formula is not approved.

**HISTORICAL CELESTITE PRICE REFERENCE (SUPERSEDED NOVEMBER 12, 2026):** For a comparable armor class, coverage and Quality V item, Celestite armor's **LISTED DISPLAY PRICE is 5× the nominal Adamantite armor price**, marked with an asterisk **'*'** immediately clarifying that **Celestite CANNOT BE BOUGHT** and its exceptional Centaur + triune divine crafting requirements MUST be satisfied before such an artifact can be forged. This is a comparative reference valuation only, not an actual Gem market sale, conventional crafting recipe, stock availability or evidence of ordinary buyback economics. No numeric Adamantite armor Gem price is fabricated by this ruling.

**OPEN FOLLOW-UP:** finalize Quality V armor AR/MR/STR/DEX/SPD benchmarks, exact MP debit and depleted-pool result, first-donning XP-snapshot persistence if item is transferred, XP rounding/floor and death/soul-zero effects, details of how overlapping weapon+armor Leydrain radii/MP cutoff behave, whether MP limits affect active channeling across the field boundary, and whether an experience-loss event also impacts non-level XP accounting. No redefinition of the other eleven pure materials or author-approved Celestite weapon effects.
## Thornspring stable Springload and construct-engineering backbone approved — November 10, 2026 (LATEST)

**LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL:** Thornspring's ONE charge per suit can be wound to full by wearer/ally spending one turn or restored by a genuine incoming qualifying physical impact (provisional threshold ~70% of quality-matched generalist Solid-wound Power). Charges **persist indefinitely whether acquired in or out of combat** until spent, including across encounter boundaries. Working Quality V Full cover: **Light AR25/STR8/DEX16/MR0, burst +50% SPD; Medium AR55/STR12/DEX26/MR0, +75%; Heavy AR90/STR15/DEX36/MR0, +35%**, all for one eligible dodge/burst maneuver, never passive SPD. Thornspring is a reliable elastic **backbone of construct engineering** without giving constructs free armor abilities. Quality I–IV figures and exact physical recharge math still open. Next remaining extraordinary material to revisit: Celestite; composites thereafter.



## November 10, 2026 — Thornspring numerical burst benchmarks approved; stable permanent charges and construct engineering role (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**EXPLICIT AUTHOR ACCEPTANCE:** Author approves Quill's immediately preceding Thornspring recommendations INCLUDING the previously proposed **one Springload charge capacity per ordinary suit**, class-specific one-maneuver **Light +50%, Medium +75%, Heavy +35% situational effective SPD bursts**, and the provisional **Quality V Full-coverage armor profiles Light AR25/STR8/DEX16/MR0, Medium AR55/STR12/DEX26/MR0, Heavy AR90/STR15/DEX36/MR0** as **approved working numerical calibration**. Armor is intentionally below corresponding Boughsteel physical protection, with low STR and unusually high DEX requirements; no passive speed increase, generic extra action/dodge, bypass of Trapped or use of SPD for unrelated attack Power. The burst applies only to the qualifying dodge/evasive movement maneuver and expends its one charge; preserves existing action economy and quality-reduced gear requirements under global rules. The working suggested **physical-hit recharge criterion about 70% of a Quality-matched generalist's Solid-wound Power threshold** is accepted as a provisional balancing target, NOT a newly resolved universal combat threshold or spell damage formula. Incoming genuine physical contact can replenish this charge if qualifying, even when armor prevented injury; the gained charge does not retroactively improve the generating attack's own defense. Exact reference-generalist builds and per-quality thresholds remain pending.

**AUTHOR'S IMPORTANT EXPLICIT CAVEAT:** Charges acquired from *incoming attacks in combat* **DO NOT DISAPPEAR** afterward. Thornspring is stable and efficient; **ALL Springload charges, whether acquired from winding or physical impacts and whether acquired in combat or beforehand, remain stored indefinitely until deliberately released/spent**, within the suit's one-charge normal capacity. No expiration at end of turn, at encounter boundary, due to injury, or after ordinary calm movement. Do not reintroduce earlier ephemeral-charge suggestion. A wearer or ally can spend one full turn operating built-in winding gear to refill it to full, including before an engagement, and need not suffer attacks to start ready. Repeated meaningful impacts can replenish charge **after it has been used**; additional impacts while fully charged do not grant extra charges or stacking speed multipliers. Injury is not a separate automatic recharge trigger; genuine force transferred through the armor is the mechanism, and being wounded under intense pressure is one dramatic way this can happen.

**CONSTRUCT ENGINEERING WORLD LORE APPROVED:** Thornspring's reliable, unusually efficient elastic mechanical energy storage/recoil management is a **major foundational material/engineering backbone of Mystarian constructs and clockwork mechanisms**. Appropriate construct designs may use Thornspring in recoil assemblies, springs, joints, energy return, locomotion, shock recovery or actuators, subject to their individual build's material/craft limits. This is substantive lore supporting the broader clockwork industry; it does **NOT** mean all constructs must be made of it, every construct gains Thornspring armor's +SPD or charge mechanic, every unit receives a free stat increase, or the material creates energy without input. Existing clockwork craft, Leywell, Cogsong and stat rules remain unchanged absent separate approval.

**STILL OPEN:** Quality I–IV Thornspring AR, STR/DEX and movement effects, exact percent/damage benchmark implementation after building reference generalists, class-specific burst movement reach/route, integration with reaction/initiative and any construct-specific designs/stat bonuses. The one-charge/three-class Quality V profiles are current working canon, subject to future balance corrections. Glassweave has already received its pure-material concept and breakage treatment; among the named twelve materials Celestite is the outstanding extraordinary/divine exception to ordinary material armor calibration. Advanced composites remain separate follow-up from pure-material identities and must not silently erase past design decisions.
## Thornspring springload stored until spent; full-turn winding; meaningful-hit recharge — November 10, 2026 (LATEST)

**AUTHOR APPROVED:** Wearer OR ally can use Thornspring's **winding gear for one full turn** to refill all its existing kinetic charge slots, before battle or midcombat. Stored Springload **does not expire**. Actual meaningful physical armor-contacting strikes can also recharge it, using a **Quality-matched generalist impact threshold philosophy** inspired by Hearthiron; no requirement to be actually wounded, unlike Bellcopper. No self-charge from harmless movements/contrived taps. Approved identity: sudden SPD bursts, especially Medium evasive armor; physical AR below Boughsteel but useful, MR0, low STR and high DEX requirements. **Pending:** numerical burst +SPD, class AR/STR/DEX, charge count, precise hit threshold (70% only proposed) and combat timing. See Smithcraft, Equipment, Combat, Changelog.



## November 10, 2026 — Thornspring stored readiness, manual winding and impact recharge (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**APPROVED THORNSPRING SPRINGLOAD POLICY:** Thornspring armor has a physical **winding gear**. **Either the wearer or a willing ally can spend one full turn activating/operating it to restore its currently available Springload charge capacity to FULL** (i.e., fill charge slots, not increase their maximum count). A suitably prepared suit can enter combat fully wound; no repeated jumps, staged falls, ally attacks, fake-threat gymnastics, or other contrived antics are required. Stored Springload **does NOT expire at turn end or after a fixed number of turns**; it **remains until deliberately spent/released**. Preserving opening-turn readiness is an explicit author design objective. An ally investing their turn in a combat rewind is a meaningful tactical choice, subject to ordinary access/position/action constraints. There is no automatic free extra attack, dodge, or turn from winding.

**APPROVED SECONDARY RECHARGE ROUTE:** In combat, **actually being struck by a meaningful physical impact** can restore Springload without operating the winding gear. Reuse the **quality-matched reference-generalist wounding benchmark / relative impact threshold philosophy discussed for Hearthiron's fire recharge**, translated into appropriate physical-impact Power; do NOT require the wearer to take an actual wound to benefit, and do NOT reuse Bellcopper's distinct actual-post-defense-wound charging trigger. The impact must genuinely contact and load the springs; trivial harmless taps, staged motions, attack attempts that fully miss, psychic effects and energy-only attacks without physical impact do not qualify. The recharge cannot retroactively alter defense against the strike which generated it. No extra charging from routine noncombat motion.

**AUTHOR-APPROVED THORNSPRING SPECIALTY DIRECTION FROM CURRENT CONVERSATION:** This material should grant **brief powerful SPD bursts rather than passive SPD bonuses**, optimizing dodges/evasive positional defense; especially **Medium** should gain a large situational SPD boost on releasing Springload. Its normal physical AR is **lower than Boughsteel but not negligible**; **intrinsic MR0**. It has **low STR support requirement near conventional Boughsteel DEX handling requirement** and unusually high DEX handling requirement near Boughsteel's STR requirement at corresponding class/quality, since controlled elastic acceleration is skill-intensive. Existing global armor/gear shortfall rules apply. Do NOT invent a generic static passive SPD increase or free automatic dodge, nor allow burst to bypass actual Trapped/no-dodge conditions, create attacks, change initiative retroactively or affect unrelated weapon Power.

**NOT YET APPROVED NUMERIC PARAMETERS:** Earlier Quill proposed full-coverage Quality V Thornspring **Light AR25/STR8/DEX16, Medium AR55/STR12/DEX26, Heavy AR90/STR15/DEX36; intrinsic MR0 for all**, and Springload **one-charge** burst SPD bonuses of **Light +50%, Medium +75%, Heavy +35%**. These specific AR/STR/DEX and +SPD percentages are **unapproved placeholders**, not numeric canon. The proposed **70% of quality-matched reference generalist's Solid-wound Power threshold** as physical impact recharge is also still an **unapproved quantitative suggestion**; the benchmark should not be applied using a spell/elemental MR formula as if it were finalized physical Power. Exact charge-capacity by class, gain-per-hit, overcharging behavior, effects of shield-only hits, whether emergency ally winding needs a specific position, and timing of actual dodge spending are pending. Preserve earlier canon Thornspring extraordinary elasticity and recoil management.

Active burst uses charges. Exact burst radius/Power/recharge math still proposed. Next material: Thornspring.



## November 9, 2026 — Hearthiron low-output warmth preserves combat charges (LATEST EXPLICIT AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**AUTHOR APPROVES QUILL'S MOST RECENT RECOMMENDATION:** Hearthiron armor, including **Medium and Heavy**, can provide sustained **ordinary low-output radiant warmth without consuming a discrete Furnace Burst combat charge** while the armor has usable background retained heat. This is an intentionally negligible long-duration draw relative to a battle-scale offensive thermal discharge, NOT an exemption from finite heat storage forever and NOT permission to provide unlimited large-area extreme heating. **Medium Furnace Burst remains one charge; Heavy remains two**, each at equal Quality-based damage. Offensive burst costs a charge, high-output active effects meaningfully spend heat, extreme heat/pyromancy can replenish in combat, campfire replenishment is slower and unspecified, and high-output thermal leakage remains dangerous. Light remains the personal warmth/survival specialist.
